-complete.
Namely, fix some
(\xi_{Y},\xi_{X})\in T_{\mathscr{A}^{(l)}}
,
w_{d}\in M_{d}
, and suppose the assumptions
jet_{N}Exp
,
jet_{N}Ln
of
§
2.5
hold for
N=\lceil\frac{d-ord(f)}{l}\rceil+1
. Then we get the statement:

(28)

\xi_{Y}(y)|_{f}-\xi_{X}(f)\ \lower 1.72218pt\hbox{$\mathrel{\mathop{\equiv}\limits^{mod\ M_{d+l}}}$}\ w_{d}\quad\quad\text{ if and only if }\quad\quad jet_{N}(e^{-\xi_{X}})jet_{N}(e^{\xi_{Y}})f\ \lower 1.72218pt\hbox{$\mathrel{\mathop{\equiv}\limits^{mod\ M_{d+l}}}$}\ f+w_{d}.

3.6.
Critical/singular/instability loci and the annihilator of
\boldsymbol{T^{1}_{\mathscr{G}}f}

Fix a group
\mathscr{G}=\mathscr{R},\mathscr{A},{\mathscr{K}}\!
and a map
f\in Maps(X,\!Y)={\mathfrak{m}}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}.
Take the image tangent space
T_{\mathscr{G}}f\subseteq R^{\oplus p}_{X}
, §
3.2
.
Take the quotient module
T^{1}_{\mathscr{G}}f:={\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$R^{\oplus p}_{X}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$T_{\mathscr{G}}f$}\right.}
. (In
[
Kerner.22
]
we show that this is the tangent
space to the
\mathscr{G}
-miniversal unfolding, when
f
is
\mathscr{G}
-finite.)
The standard way to measure
how large is this tangent space goes via the support of
T^{1}_{\mathscr{G}}f
, i.e. the annihilator of this quotient module

(29)

\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{G}}:=Ann[T^{1}_{\mathscr{G}}f]=\{q\in R_{X}|\ q\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq T_{\mathscr{G}}f\}\subseteq R_{X}.

This is the largest ideal satisfying:
T_{\mathscr{G}}f\supseteq\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{G}}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.

Fix a filtration
I^{\bullet}\subset R_{X}
and the corresponding filtrations
\mathscr{G}^{(\bullet)}
,
T_{\mathscr{G}^{(\bullet)}}
, see §
3.3
.
One gets the filtered annihilators
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{G}^{(j)}}:=Ann[T^{1}_{\mathscr{G}^{(j)}}f]\subseteq R_{X}
for
j\geq 0
.

Definition 3.12
.

​1.​​

For
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{G}}\subset R_{X}
the scheme-germ
V(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{G}})\subseteq X
is called the critical (
Crit(f)
),
resp. the singular (
Sing(f)
), resp. the instability locus of
f
.

​2.​​

The map
f
is called
\mathscr{G}
-finite if
\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{G}}}\supseteq{\mathfrak{m}}
, i.e. geometrically
V(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{G}})=o\in X
or
V(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{G}})=\varnothing.

If
X
is smooth,
\Bbbk={\bar{\Bbbk}},
and
f_{1},\dots,f_{p}\in R_{X}
is a regular sequence then (set-theoretically)
Sing(f)=Sing(V(f))
, the singular locus of the complete intersection.
Otherwise the two objects can differ.

Remark 3.13
.

Classically the Fitting (determinantal) ideals of the modules
T^{1}_{\mathscr{R}}f
,
T^{1}_{\mathscr{K}}\!f
were used. We use the annihilator ideals in (
29
)
for two reasons:

•

These annihilators appear naturally in the criteria for group-orbits, §
4.1
, §
5.1
, §
7.2
.

•

The ideals
Fitt_{\bullet}(T^{1}_{\mathscr{A}}f)
are
not immediately defined, as
T^{1}_{\mathscr{A}}f
is not an
R_{X}
module, while as
R_{Y}
-module it is not finitely generated.

3.6.1.

Below we discuss the annihilators
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}},\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{K}}\!
. The annihilator
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}
is much more delicate, see §
6
.

Example 3.14
.

Let
R
be one of
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk[[x]]$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$J$}\right.}
,
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk\{x\}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$J$}\right.}
,
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk\langle x\rangle$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$J$}\right.}
, see §
2.1
.ii.

i.

For
p\!=\!1
and
R
one of
\Bbbk[[x]],\Bbbk\{x\},\Bbbk\langle x\rangle
the annihilators are just the Jacobian and the Tjurina ideals,

T^{1}_{\mathscr{R}}f={\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$R$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$Jac(f)$}\right.},\quad\quad\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}=Jac(f),\quad\quad T^{1}_{\mathscr{K}}\!f={\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$R$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$(f)+Jac(f)$}\right.},\quad\quad\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{K}}\!=Jac(f)+(f).

Here (when
\Bbbk
is not a field) the derivatives are taken with respect to
x
-variables only.

For
J\neq 0
one gets the Bruce-Roberts versions of the Jacobian/Tjurina ideals,
[
Bru.Rob.88
]
.

ii.

For
p\geq 2
the annihilators are not computed easily, but one has immediate bounds via the determinantal ideals of the generating matrix
[\xi_{i}(f_{j})]
of
T_{\mathscr{R}}f
.
(For the regular rings
\Bbbk[[x]]
,
\Bbbk\{x\}
,
\Bbbk\langle x\rangle
, this is just the matrix of partials,
[\partial_{x_{i}}f_{j}]
.)
One has (see e.g.
[
Eisenbud
, §20]
):

(30)

I_{p}[\xi_{i}(f_{j})]\subseteq\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}\subseteq\sqrt{I_{p}[\xi_{i}(f_{j})]},\quad\quad\quad\quad I_{p}[\xi_{i}(f_{j})]+(f)\subseteq\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{K}}\!\subseteq\sqrt{I_{p}[\xi_{i}(f_{j})]+(f)}.

iii.

In particular one gets:
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}+(f)\subseteq\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{K}}\!\subseteq\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}+(f)}
. Hence
Sing(f)=Crit(f)\cap V(f)
.

iv.

Let
p\geq 2
. In many cases one has
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f
. For example, this holds if
the determinantal ideal
I_{p}[\xi_{i}(f_{j})]\subset R_{X}
is radical, as then
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}=I_{p}[\xi_{i}(f_{j})]
.

v.

Take the filtration
I^{\bullet}\subset R_{X}
. One has
I^{j+1}\cdot\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{G}}\subseteq\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{G}^{(j)}}\subseteq\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{G}}
.

vi.

(The case of unfolding) Take a map
f:(X\times\Bbbk^{r}_{u},o)\to(\Bbbk^{p}_{y}\times\Bbbk^{r}_{u},o)
,
(x,u)\to(f_{o}(x)+g(x,u),u)
, where
(g(x,u))\subseteq(x)\cdot(u)
.
Then
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{K}}\!(f)=\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{K}}\!(f_{o})+(u)
and
\mathfrak{a}_{{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(0)}}(f)=\mathfrak{a}_{{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(0)}}(f_{o})+(u)
.

3.6.2.
Maps of finite singularity type

The following criterion is well known for
\Bbbk=\mathbb{C}
and
X
-ICIS, see e.g.
[
Mond-Montaldi.91
]
,
[
Mon. Nuñ.-Bal.
, pg.224]
.

Lemma 3.15
.

Let the pair
(R_{X},R_{Y})
be one of
({\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk[[x]]$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$J$}\right.},\Bbbk[[y]])
,
({\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk\{x\}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$J$}\right.},\Bbbk\{y\})
,
({\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk\langle x\rangle$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$J$}\right.},\Bbbk\langle y\rangle),
see §
2.1
.ii.
The following conditions are equivalent for a map
f:X\to(\Bbbk^{p},o)
.

​​
1.​

The restriction of
f
to
Crit(f)\!\to\!(\Bbbk^{p},o)
is a finite morphism, i.e. the
R_{X}
-module
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$R_{X}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}$}\right.}
is a f.g.
R_{Y}
-module;

​​
2.​

The
R_{X}
-module
T^{1}_{\mathscr{R}}f
is a f.g.
R_{Y}
-module;

​​
3.​

T^{1}_{\mathscr{K}}\!f
is a f.g.
\Bbbk
-module;   (If
\Bbbk
is a field then
f
is
{\mathscr{K}}\!
-finite.)

​​
4.​

f^{-1}(o)\cap Crit(f)
is a (fat) point over
Spec(\Bbbk)
(i.e.
\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}+(f)}\supseteq(x)
).

Moreover, if
T^{1}_{\mathscr{K}}\!f\in mod\text{-}\Bbbk
is generated by
\{v_{\bullet}\}\subset R^{\oplus p}_{X}
, then
T^{1}_{\mathscr{R}}f\in mod(R_{Y})
is generated by
\{v_{\bullet}\}
.

In this case
f
is called
a map of finite singularity type
.

Proof.
\bf 1\Rrightarrow 2.

T^{1}_{\mathscr{R}}f
is a f.g. module over
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$R_{X}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}$}\right.}
, and thus a f.g. module over
R_{Y}
.

\bf 2\Rrightarrow 3.
If ​
T^{1}_{\mathscr{R}}f
is f.g. over ​
R_{Y},
then
T^{1}_{\mathscr{R}}f\!\otimes\!{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$R_{Y}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$(y)$}\right.}
is f.g. over ​
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$R_{Y}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$(y)$}\right.}\!=\!\Bbbk.

Now observe: ​
T^{1}_{\mathscr{R}}f\!\otimes_{R_{Y}}\!{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$R_{Y}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$(y)$}\right.}\!=\!T^{1}_{\mathscr{K}}\!f.

\bf 3\Rrightarrow 4.
As
T^{1}_{\mathscr{K}}\!f
is f.g. over
\Bbbk,
it is a module over
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$R_{X}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$(x)^{d}$}\right.}
for some
d\gg 1.

Therefore
\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{K}}\!}=\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}+(f)}\supseteq(x)
. Thus
f^{-1}(o)\cap Crit(f)
is a fat point over
Spec(\Bbbk).

\bf 4\Rrightarrow 1.
By the assumption
\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}+(f)}\supseteq(x).
Hence the map
Crit(f)\to(\Bbbk^{p},o)
is quasi-finite.
The finiteness follows by the Weierstraß-finiteness,
2.3
.iii.

\blacksquare

4.
Criteria for right orbits, “
\mathscr{R}f
vs
T_{\mathscr{R}}f
”

Let
R_{X}
be one of the rings
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk[[x]]$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$J$}\right.}
,
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk\{x\}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$J$}\right.}
,
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk\langle x\rangle$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$J$}\right.}
, with
\Bbbk
a local ring, see §
2.1
.ii.

4.1.
The
\mathscr{R}
-implicit function theorem

Take two ideals
\mathfrak{a}\subseteq I\subseteq R_{X}
, with
\mathfrak{a}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}
, and a map-germ
f\in I\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.
(The usual choice is:
I=R_{X}
or
I={\mathfrak{m}}
or
I=\sqrt{(f)}
.)
For the notation
I^{ord(f)-2}
see §
2.1
.v.

Theorem 4.1
.

Suppose the ideal
\mathfrak{a}
satisfies
\mathfrak{a}^{2}\cdot I^{ord(f)-2}\!\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot\mathfrak{a}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f.

​1.​​

(The case
J=0
)
Then
\{f\}+\mathfrak{a}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f\subseteq\mathscr{R}f.

​2.​​

(The case
J\neq 0,
assume the
jet_{0}
-condition of §
2.5
.)
Then
\{f\}+\mathfrak{a}^{2}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f\subseteq\mathscr{R}f.

A remark: the assumption
\mathfrak{a}^{2}\cdot I^{ord(f)-2}\!\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot\mathfrak{a}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f
is preserved by
\mathscr{R}
-equivalence.

The automorphism
\Phi_{X}\!\in\!Aut_{X}
(constructed in the proof) satisfies:
\Phi(x)\!-\!x\!\in\!\mathfrak{a},
resp. ​(for part 2)
\Phi(x)\!-\!x\!\in\!\mathfrak{a}^{2}.

Proof.

1.

Take a perturbation
g\in\mathfrak{a}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f
. We want to resolve the condition
\Phi_{X}(f)=f+g
, where
\Phi_{X}\in\mathscr{R}=Aut_{X}
.
Fix some (finite sets of) generators
\{\xi_{i}(f)\}
of the
R_{X}
-module
\mathfrak{a}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f=\mathfrak{a}\cdot Der_{X}(f)
.
Expand
g=\sum c^{g}_{i}\xi_{j}(f)
, here
c^{g}_{i}\in R_{X}
.

We look for the coordinate change
\Phi_{X}\in Aut_{X}
in the form
x\to x+\sum c_{i}\xi_{i}(x)
.
Thus we want to resolve the equation

(31)

f(x+\sum c_{i}\xi_{i}(x))=f(x)+\sum c^{g}_{i}\cdot\xi_{i}(f),\quad\text{ with the unknowns $\{c_{*}\}$}.

Taylor-expand the left hand side:

f(x)+\sum c_{i}\xi_{i}(f)+H_{\geq 2}(\{c_{i}\xi_{i}(x)\})
.
Here
H_{\geq 2}(z)\in\sum^{ord(f)}_{l\geq 2}I^{ord(f)-l}\cdot(z)^{l}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.
As
\mathfrak{a}\subseteq I
one has:
H_{\geq 2}(\mathfrak{a}\cdot z)\in I^{ord(f)-2}\cdot(\mathfrak{a}\cdot z)^{2}\cdot R_{X}[[\mathfrak{a}\cdot z]][z]
.
These are polynomials in
z
whose coefficients are (formal/analytic/algebraic)
power series in
\mathfrak{a}\cdot z
, see §
2.1
.iv. Therefore:

(32)

H_{\geq 2}(\{c_{i}\xi_{i}(x)\})\in(\{c_{*}\})^{2}\cdot\mathfrak{a}^{2}\cdot I^{ord(f)-2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq(\{c_{*}\})^{2}\cdot{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot\mathfrak{a}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f.

Thus we can present
H_{\geq 2}(c_{i}\xi_{i}(x))=\sum h_{i}(c_{*})\xi_{i}(f)
, for some (fixed) power series

h_{i}(z)\in(z)^{2}\cdot{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot R_{X}[[\mathfrak{a}\cdot z]][z]
.
These (formal/analytic/algebraic) series
h_{i}
satisfy:
h_{i}(R_{X})\subset{\mathfrak{m}}
.

Thus to resolve the equation (
31
) it is enough to resolve the (finite) system of equations:

\{c_{i}+h_{i}(c_{*})=c^{g}_{i}\}_{i}
.
And now apply the
IFT_{1\hskip-2.84544pt\rm I}
, see §
2.3
.i, to get the solutions
c_{*}(x)\in R_{X}
.

Finally we take the coordinate change
\Phi_{X}:x\to x+\sum c_{i}(x)\xi_{i}(x)
.
It is invertible (as
\mathfrak{a}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}
), and
defines the needed automorphism
\Phi_{X}\in Aut_{X}
.

2.

Take a perturbation
g\in\mathfrak{a}^{2}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f.
By part 1
we have a map
\Phi_{X}:x\to x+\xi(x)
, with
\xi\in\mathfrak{a}^{2}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}
, satisfying
\Phi^{-1}_{X}(f+g)=f
. As
J\neq 0
, this
\Phi_{X}

is not necessarily a “coordinate change” on
X
, i.e. not an automorphism of
R_{X}
. By the
jet_{0}
-assumption we can extend
\Phi_{X}
to an automorphism
\Psi_{X}\in Aut_{X}
, satisfying

\Psi_{X}(x)-x-\xi(x)\in\mathfrak{a}^{3}
. Then
\Psi^{-1}_{X}(f+g)-f\in\mathfrak{a}^{3}\cdot I^{ord(f)-1}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq\mathfrak{a}^{2}\cdot{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f.

(Note that we cannot assume
\Psi^{-1}_{X}(f+g)-f\in\mathfrak{a}^{3}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f,
because
\Psi_{X}-Id-\xi
is not a derivation of
R_{X}
.)

Iterate this argument to get:
\Psi_{X,d}^{-1}(f+g)-f\in\mathfrak{a}^{3}\cdot{\mathfrak{m}}^{d}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
for each
d\geq 1
.
Hence
f+g\in\overline{\mathscr{R}f}
, the orbit-closure in the
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\bullet}
-filtration topology.
The statement follows now by lemma
3.6
.

\blacksquare

Example 4.2
.

Let
p\!\geq\!2
and
R_{X}
be one of
\Bbbk[[x]],\Bbbk\{x\},\Bbbk\langle x\rangle
, ​see §
2.1
.ii.
​Take
I\!={\mathfrak{m}}
and
(\Bbbk^{n},o)\!\ \lower 1.72218pt\hbox{$\mathrel{\mathop{\to}\limits^{f}}$}\ \!(\Bbbk^{p},o).

i.

Suppose
f
is a submersion, i.e.
T_{\mathscr{R}}f=R^{\oplus p}_{X}
. (Thus
p\leq n
.)
Then for
\mathfrak{a}={\mathfrak{m}}^{2}
one gets
\mathscr{R}f\supseteq\{f\}+{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.
In particular one gets the normal form of submersion,
f\ \lower 1.72218pt\hbox{$\mathrel{\mathop{\sim}\limits^{\mathscr{R}}}$}\ (x_{1},\dots,x_{p})
.

ii.

More generally, the theorem gives:
If
\mathfrak{a}^{2}\!\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot\mathfrak{a}\cdot\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}

then
\mathscr{R}f\supseteq\{f\}\!+\mathfrak{a}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f
.

(Here
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}
is the annihilator of the critical locus, §
3.6
.)
E.g. one has
\mathscr{R}f\supseteq\{f\}\!+{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot\mathfrak{a}^{2}_{\mathscr{R}}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.

iii.

In many cases one has
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f
, see example
3.14
.iv.
Then theorem
4.1
gives:
\mathscr{R}f\supseteq\{f\}+\mathfrak{a}^{2}_{\mathscr{R}}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.
Geometrically:
f
is determined up to
\mathscr{R}
-equivalence by its 2-jet on the critical locus
Crit(f)=V(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}})
, taken with its annihilator structure.

4.2.
More examples for the case of one power series, i.e.
\boldsymbol{p=1}

Let
R_{X}
be one of
\Bbbk[[x]]
,
\Bbbk\{x\}
,
\Bbbk\langle x\rangle
. Take
f\in{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}
,
I={\mathfrak{m}}
and
\mathfrak{a}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}
. Theorem
4.1
reads:

(33)

If
\mathfrak{a}^{2}\cdot{\mathfrak{m}}^{ord(f)-2}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot\mathfrak{a}\cdot Jac(f)

then
\{f\}+\mathfrak{a}\cdot Jac(f)\subseteq\mathscr{R}f
.

Below we use a simpler though more restrictive condition:
\mathfrak{a}\cdot{\mathfrak{m}}^{ord(f)-2}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot Jac(f)
.
Then we get:

Corollary 4.3
.

1.

\mathscr{R}f\supseteq\{f\}+Jac(f)\cdot\Big(({\mathfrak{m}}\cdot Jac(f)):{\mathfrak{m}}^{ord(f)-2}\Big)
.

2.

Take the smallest
d\in\mathbb{N}
satisfying
{\mathfrak{m}}^{2d+ord(f)-2}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}^{d+1}\cdot Jac(f)
. Then
\mathscr{R}f\supseteq\{f\}+{\mathfrak{m}}^{d}\cdot Jac(f)
.

Example 4.4
.

i.

Obviously
({\mathfrak{m}}\cdot Jac(f)):{\mathfrak{m}}^{ord(f)-2}\supseteq{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot Jac(f)
. Therefore:
\{f\}+{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot Jac(f)^{2}\subseteq\mathscr{R}f
.
This is well known for
R_{X}=\mathbb{C}\{x\}
, e.g.
[
Ruiz
, lemma 2.2, pg.91]
, or for
char(\Bbbk)=0
,
[
Kucharz.86
]
.

In particular, if
\sqrt{Jac(f)}={\mathfrak{m}}
then
f
is finitely-determined.

More generally, in this way one can extend many other results of
[
Kucharz.86
]
. We omit the details.

ii.

(Morse lemma)

Let
\Bbbk
be a field,
f\in{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}
, and
rank[f^{\prime\prime}|_{o}]=r
. Then
f\ \lower 1.72218pt\hbox{$\mathrel{\mathop{\sim}\limits^{\mathscr{R}}}$}\ Q_{2}(x)+{\tilde{f}}(w)
, where
x=(x_{1},\dots,x_{r})
,
w=(w_{1},\dots,w_{n-r})
,

{\tilde{f}}(w)\in(w)^{3}
(is independent of
x
)
and
Q_{2}(x)
is a homogeneous quadratic polynomial.

Moreover, for
\Bbbk=\bar{\Bbbk}
one can diagonalize the quadratic form
Q_{2}
to get:
f\ \lower 1.72218pt\hbox{$\mathrel{\mathop{\sim}\limits^{\mathscr{R}}}$}\ \sum x^{2}_{i}+{\tilde{f}}(w)
.

Proof.
Apply a
GL(n,\Bbbk)
transformation to get
f\!-\!Q_{2}(x)\!\in\!(x,w)^{3}
. Then
Jac(f)\!+\!(x,w)^{2}\!\supset\!(x_{1},\dots,x_{r})
.
And thus we have
f-Q_{2}(x)\in(x,w)\cdot Jac(f)^{2}+(w)^{3}
. The statement follows now by part i.

\blacksquare

iii.

Assuming
ord(f)\geq 3
one has
({\mathfrak{m}}\cdot Jac(f)):{\mathfrak{m}}^{ord(f)-2}\supseteq Jac(f)
.
Then we get:
\{f\}+Jac(f)^{2}\subseteq\mathscr{R}f
. For
R_{X}=\mathbb{C}\{x\}
this is Lemma 3.20 in
[
Ebeling
]
. Its proof is based on vector field integration,
thus cannot be used when
\Bbbk
is an arbitrary field.

iv.

If
ord(f)=2
then by Morse lemma we split the variables,
f=Q_{2}(x)+{\tilde{f}}(w)
with
{\tilde{f}}(w)\in(w)^{3}
. Then one has:

\mathscr{R}f\supseteq\{f\}+(x)^{2}\cdot(x,w)+(x)\cdot Jac({\tilde{f}}(w))+Jac({\tilde{f}}(w))^{2}
.

v.

To show how this corollary strengthens the classical bounds, let
\Bbbk
be a field of characteristic zero, and take

f
with an ordinary multiple point at
o\in(\Bbbk^{n},o)
.
This means: the partials
\partial_{1}f,\dots,\partial_{n}f
form a regular sequence, and their leading terms (of order
ord(f)-1
) form a regular sequence as well.
Let
q
be the smallest integer satisfying

{\mathfrak{m}}^{q}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\cdot Jac(f)
. Then
q=n\cdot(ord(f)-2)+1
, see exercise 2.2.7 in
[
Gr.Lo.Sh
]
. The known determinacy bound is:

\{f\}+{\mathfrak{m}}^{q}\subset\mathscr{R}f
, see §5 of
[
B.G.K.22
]
.

We claim:

\mathscr{R}f\supseteq\{f\}+{\mathfrak{m}}^{\lceil\frac{q-ord(f)}{2}\rceil}\cdot Jac(f)
. (This bound is better, even asymptotically.)

Proof.
As
f
has an ordinary multiple point we have:
{\mathfrak{m}}^{q}\!=\!Jac(f)\!\cdot\!{\mathfrak{m}}^{q-ord(f)+1}
. Assume
ord(f)\!\geq\!2+\frac{3}{n-1}
and denote

d\!:=\lceil\frac{q-ord(f)}{2}\rceil\!+\!1
. Take
\mathfrak{a}\!=\!{\mathfrak{m}}^{d}\!\subset\!I\!=\!{\mathfrak{m}}
. To use part 2 of the corollary we verify:

(34)

\mathfrak{a}^{2}\cdot{\mathfrak{m}}^{ord(f)-2}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot\mathfrak{a}\cdot Jac(f),\quad\quad\text{ i.e. }\quad\quad{\mathfrak{m}}^{2d+ord(f)-2}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}^{d+1}\cdot Jac(f).

Indeed, by our assumption:

(35)

2d+ord(f)-2\geq q\quad\text{ and }\quad(d+1)+(ord(f)-1)=\lceil\frac{q-ord(f)}{2}\rceil+1\leq q.\hskip 56.9055pt{\hfill\blacksquare}

vi.

Take the
E_{6}
singularity,
f(x,y)=x^{3}+y^{4}
. Assume
\Bbbk
is a field,
char(\Bbbk)\neq 2,3
. Denote
{\mathfrak{m}}=(x,y)
,
\mathfrak{a}={\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\subset I={\mathfrak{m}}
.
We get:
\mathfrak{a}^{2}\cdot{\mathfrak{m}}^{ord(f)-1}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot\mathfrak{a}\cdot Jac(f)
. Therefore
\mathscr{R}f\supseteq\{f\}+{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\cdot(x^{2},y^{3})
.
The ideal
{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\cdot(x^{2},y^{3})
contains all the monomials lying above the Newton diagram of
x^{3}+y^{4}
. Compare this result to the
standard determinacy statement:
\mathscr{R}f\supseteq\{f\}+{\mathfrak{m}}^{5}
.

4.3.
The filtration criterion for
\mathscr{R}
-orbits

Take the filtration ​
M_{\bullet}\!\!:=\!I^{\bullet}\!\cdot\!R^{\oplus p}_{X}.
Fix some integers
1\!\leq\!j\!<\!d.

Theorem 4.5
.

Suppose the conditions
jet_{N}(Exp)
,
jet_{N}(Ln)
of §
2.5

hold for
N=\lceil\frac{d-ord(f)}{j}\rceil
. Then:

{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}f}\supseteq\{f\}+I^{d}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}

if and only if

{T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}}f}\supseteq I^{d}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.

Proof.
The part
\boldsymbol{``\Rrightarrow"}
.
First we prove:
T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}}f+M_{d+1}\supseteq M_{d}
.
Let
w\in M_{d}
, then
gf\equiv f+w\ mod\ M_{d+1}
for some
g\in\mathscr{R}^{(j)}
. Use the assumption
jet_{N}(Ln)
to get the derivation

-\sum^{N}_{i=1}\frac{({1\hskip-2.84544pt\rm I}-g)^{i}}{i}+\xi_{g}\in T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}}
, for some

\xi_{g}\in End^{(Nj+1)}_{\Bbbk}(R)
. And then

(36)

T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}}f\ni\Big(-\sum^{N}_{i=1}\frac{({1\hskip-2.84544pt\rm I}-g)^{i}}{i}+\xi_{g}\Big)f\equiv w\ mod(M_{d+1}+M_{Nj+1+ord(f)})\equiv w\ mod\ M_{d+1}.

Here
Nj+ord(f)\geq d
because of the assumption
N=\lceil\frac{d-ord(f)}{j}\rceil
.

Therefore we have
T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}}f+M_{d+1}\supseteq M_{d}
. As
M_{d+1}=I\cdot M_{d}
, we get (by Nakayama)
T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}}f\supseteq M_{d}
.

The part
\boldsymbol{``\Lleftarrow"}
.

First we prove:
\mathscr{R}^{(j+k)}f+M_{d+k+1}\supseteq\{f\}+M_{d+k}
for every
k\geq 0
. Indeed,
T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}}f\supseteq M_{d}
implies

T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j+k)}}f\supseteq M_{d+k}
for every
k\geq 0
. (Using
M_{\bullet}=I^{\bullet}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.)
Accordingly we present an element
w\in M_{d+k}
as
w=\xi(f)
, for some
\xi\in T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j+k)}}
.
The condition
jet_{N}(Exp)
ensures an element
\Phi:=\sum^{N}_{i=0}\frac{\xi^{i}}{i!}+\phi_{\xi}\in\mathscr{R}^{(j+k)}
, where

\phi_{\xi}\in End^{(N(j+k)+1)}_{\Bbbk}(R_{X})
. And then

\Phi(f)-f-w\in M_{d+k+1}+M_{(j+k)N+ord(f)+1}.

Finally use
(j+k)N+ord(f)\geq d+k
to get

\Phi(f)-f-w\in M_{d+k+1}
.

Iterating
\mathscr{R}^{(j+k)}f+M_{d+k+1}\supseteq\{f\}+M_{d+k}
for
k\geq 0
we get:
\overline{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}f}\supseteq\{f\}+M_{d}.

Now apply part 2 of Lemma
3.6
.

\blacksquare

Corollary 4.6
.

​(
R_{X}
is one of
{\Bbbk[[x]]},{\Bbbk\{x\}},{\Bbbk\langle x\rangle}
, with
\Bbbk
a ​local ring, ​see ​§
2.1
.ii.) Take
I\!\subseteq\!(x)
​and ​
f\!\in\!(x)\!\cdot\!R^{\oplus p}_{X}.

​1.

Suppose
2,3,\dots,N\in\Bbbk^{\times}
for
N\!=\!\lceil\frac{d-ord(f)}{j}\rceil.

Then:

\mathscr{R}^{(j)}f\supseteq\{f\}+I^{d}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
if and only if
T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}}f\supseteq I^{d}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.

​2.

Suppose
2\in\Bbbk^{\times}
. If
T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}}f\supseteq I^{d}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}

then
\mathscr{R}^{(d-j-ord(f))}f\supseteq\{f\}+I^{2d-2j-ord(f)}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.

Proof.
1.

Combine theorem
4.5
with lemma
3.6
.
The
jet_{N}
-condition holds by example
2.1
.

2.
We get
T_{\mathscr{R}^{(d-j-ord(f))}}f\supseteq I^{2d-2j-ord(f)}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.
Now apply part one with
N:=\lceil\frac{2d-2j-2ord(f)}{d-j-ord(f)}\rceil=2
.

\blacksquare

Example 4.7
.

i.

Take
p=1
,
I={\mathfrak{m}}
,
j=1
and suppose
\Bbbk
is a field, with
char(\Bbbk)=0
or
char(\Bbbk)>d-ord(f)
. Then:

\mathscr{R}^{(1)}f\supseteq\{f\}+{\mathfrak{m}}^{d}
if and only if
{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\cdot Jac(f)\supseteq{\mathfrak{m}}^{d}
.

In particular, the
\mathscr{R}
-order of determinacy of
f
is
\leq\mu(f)+1
.
In
char(\Bbbk)=0
this is well known, e.g.
[
Gr.Lo.Sh
, Corollary 2.24]
.
For
char(\Bbbk)>d-ord(f)
this improves the known bound,
[
Bou.Gr.Ma.12
]
,
[
Greuel-Pham.19
]
:
the
\mathscr{R}
-order of determinacy of
f
is
\leq 2\mu(f)-ord(f)+2
.

ii.

In part two of corollary
4.6
the conclusion is weaker, but also the assumption is weaker.
For
j=1
this part two is known, e.g. see corollary 5.2 of
[
B.G.K.22
]
.

5.
Criteria for contact orbits, “
{\mathscr{K}}\!f
vs
T_{\mathscr{K}}\!f
”

Let
R_{X}
be one of the rings
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk[[x]]$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$J$}\right.},\!{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk\{x\}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$J$}\right.},\!{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk\langle x\rangle$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$J$}\right.},
with
\Bbbk
a local ring, see §
2.1
.ii.

5.1.
The
{\mathscr{K}}\!
-implicit function theorem

Take two ideals
\mathfrak{a}\subseteq I\subseteq R_{X}
, with
\mathfrak{a}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}
, and a map-germ
f\in I\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.
(The usual choice is
I=R_{X},

I={\mathfrak{m}}
or
I=\sqrt{(f)}
.) For the notation
I^{ord(f)-2}
see §
2.1
.v.

Theorem 5.1
.

Suppose

\mathfrak{a}^{2}\cdot I^{ord(f)-2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot\mathfrak{a}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f+{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot(f)\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}.

​1.​​

(The case
J=0
.) Then

\{f\}+(\mathfrak{a}^{2}\cdot I^{ord(f)-2}+{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot(f))\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq{\mathscr{K}}\!f
.

​2.​​

(The case
J\neq 0,
assume the
jet_{0}
-condition of §
2.5
.)
Then

\{f\}+(\mathfrak{a}^{3}\cdot I^{ord(f)-2}+{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot(f))\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq{\mathscr{K}}\!f
.

A remark: the assumption
\mathfrak{a}^{2}\cdot I^{ord(f)-2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot\mathfrak{a}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f+{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot(f)\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
is preserved by
{\mathscr{K}}\!
-equivalence.

Proof.

1.

Take a perturbation
g\in(\mathfrak{a}^{2}\cdot I^{ord(f)-2}+{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot(f))\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
. We want to resolve the condition
\Phi_{X}(f)=({1\hskip-2.84544pt\rm I}+U)\cdot(f+g)
, where
\Phi_{X}\in Aut_{X}

and
U\in Mat_{p\times p}({\mathfrak{m}})
.
As in the proof of Theorem
4.1
we fix some (finite) sets of generators

\{\xi_{i}(f)\}
of
\mathfrak{a}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f\subseteq R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.

Present the coordinate change in the form
\Phi_{X}:x\to x+\sum c_{i}\xi_{i}(x)
, here
\{c_{i}\}
are unknowns.

•

As in the
\mathscr{R}
-case we get:

\Phi_{X}(f)-f-\sum c_{i}\xi_{i}(f)\in(\{c_{*}\})^{2}\cdot\mathfrak{a}^{2}\cdot I^{ord(f)-2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.

Therefore, as in the
\mathscr{R}
-case, we can present

(37)

\Phi_{X}(f)=f+\sum\big(c_{i}+H^{(1)}_{i}(\{c_{*}\})\big)\xi_{i}(f)+H^{(2)}(\{c_{*}\})\cdot f.

Here
H^{(1)}_{i}(z)\in{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot R_{X}[[{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot z]][z]
and
H^{(1)}_{i}({\mathfrak{m}})\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}\subset R_{X}
.
Similarly,
H^{(2)}(z)\in Mat_{p\times p}({\mathfrak{m}}\cdot R_{X}[[{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot z]][z])
and
H^{(2)}({\mathfrak{m}})\subset Mat_{p\times p}({\mathfrak{m}})
.

•

Expand
g=g_{\mathfrak{a}}+U^{g}\cdot f
, here
g_{\mathfrak{a}}\in\mathfrak{a}^{2}\cdot I^{ord(f)-2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
and
U^{g}\in Mat_{p\times p}({\mathfrak{m}})
.
Thus
({1\hskip-2.84544pt\rm I}+U)(f+g)=f+({1\hskip-2.84544pt\rm I}+U)g_{\mathfrak{a}}+(U+U^{g}+U\cdot U^{g})f.

We can present
({1\hskip-2.84544pt\rm I}+U)g_{\mathfrak{a}}=\sum_{j}l_{j}(U)\cdot\xi_{j}(f)+{\tilde{U}}^{g}\cdot f
,
where

l_{j}(U)
and
{\tilde{U}}^{g}
are linear functions in the entries of
U
.

Altogether the condition
\Phi_{X}(f)=({1\hskip-2.84544pt\rm I}+U)\cdot(f+g)
becomes:

(38)

f+\sum\big(c_{i}+H^{(1)}_{i}(\{c_{*}\})\big)\xi_{i}(f)+H^{(2)}(\{c_{*}\})\cdot f=f+\sum_{i}l_{i}(U)\cdot\xi_{i}(f)+(U+U^{g}+U\cdot U^{g}+{\tilde{U}}^{g})\cdot f.

Therefore we have transformed the condition
\Phi_{X}(f)=({1\hskip-2.84544pt\rm I}+U)(f+g)
into the system of equations:

(39)

c_{i}+H^{(1)}_{i}(\{c_{*}\})=l_{i}(U),\quad H^{(2)}(\{c_{*}\})=U+U^{g}+U\cdot U^{g}+{\tilde{U}}^{g},\quad\text{with the unknowns $\{c_{*}\},U$}.

Apply the
IFT_{1\hskip-2.84544pt\rm I}
, §
2.3
.i, to determine
c_{*}(x)\in{\mathfrak{m}}
and
U\in Mat_{p\times p}({\mathfrak{m}})
.
We get the transformation
(\Phi_{X},{1\hskip-2.84544pt\rm I}+U)\in\mathscr{R}\ltimes GL(p,R_{X})
that satisfies the condition
\Phi_{X}(f)=({1\hskip-2.84544pt\rm I}+U)\cdot(f+g)
.

2.

It is enough to prove:
{\mathscr{K}}\!f\supseteq\{f\}+\mathfrak{a}^{3}\cdot I^{ord(f)-2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
(using the
GL(p,R_{X})
-transformation).
Take a perturbation
g\in\mathfrak{a}^{3}\cdot I^{ord(f)-2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}.
The previous argument gives
a map
\Phi_{X}:x\to x+\xi(x)
, with
\xi({\mathfrak{m}})\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot\mathfrak{a}^{2}
, and a matrix
U
, satisfying
\Phi_{X}(f)=({1\hskip-2.84544pt\rm I}+U)(f+g)
.
As in the proof of theorem
4.1
this
\Phi_{X}

is not necessarily “a coordinate change” on
X
. By the
jet_{0}
-assumption we can extend
\Phi_{X}
to a coordinate change

\Psi_{X}\in Aut_{X}
, satisfying:

\Psi_{X}(x)-x-\sum c_{i}\xi_{i}(x)\in\mathfrak{a}^{3}\cdot{\mathfrak{m}}
. Then

\Psi_{X}^{-1}\big(({1\hskip-2.84544pt\rm I}+U)(f+g)\big)-f\in{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot\mathfrak{a}^{3}\cdot I^{ord(f)-2}R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.
(As in the
\mathscr{R}
-case we remark: one cannot assume
\Psi_{X}^{-1}\big(({1\hskip-2.84544pt\rm I}+U)(f+g)\big)\in\{f\}+\mathfrak{a}^{3}\cdot{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f
.)

Iterate this argument to get
f+g\ \lower 1.72218pt\hbox{$\mathrel{\mathop{\sim}\limits^{{\mathscr{K}}\!}}$}\ \{f\}+{\mathfrak{m}}^{d}\cdot\mathfrak{a}^{3}\cdot I^{ord(f)-2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
for each
d\geq 1
.
Thus
f+g\in\overline{{\mathscr{K}}\!f}
, the orbit-closure in the
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\bullet}
-filtration topology.
The statement follows now by part 2 of lemma
3.6
.

\blacksquare

Example 5.2
.

Let
R_{X}
be one of the rings
\Bbbk[[x]]
,
\Bbbk\{x\}
,
\Bbbk\langle x\rangle
, with
\Bbbk
a local ring, see §
2.1
.ii.
Take
I={\mathfrak{m}}
and assume
(f)\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}
. We get the following bounds on the orbit
{\mathscr{K}}\!f.

i.

{\mathscr{K}}\!f\supseteq\{f\}+({\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\cdot\mathfrak{a}^{2}_{\mathscr{R}}+{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot(f))\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.
For
p=1
this reads:
{\mathscr{K}}\!f\supseteq{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\cdot Jac(f)^{2}+f\cdot{\mathfrak{m}}
.

•

Suppose
f
has an isolated singularity, i.e.
Crit(f)\cap V(f)=\{o\}\in\Bbbk^{n}
, i.e.

\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}+(f)}={\mathfrak{m}}.
Then
f
is finitely-
{\mathscr{K}}\!
-determined.

•

The inclusion
{\mathscr{K}}\!f\supseteq\{f\}+({\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\cdot\mathfrak{a}^{2}_{\mathscr{R}}+{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot(f))\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
strengthens Theorem 2.1 of
[
Cut.Sri.97
]
,
which reads:

If
R_{X}=\Bbbk[[x]]
and
\mathfrak{a}=(f)+Fitt_{0}(T^{1}_{\mathscr{R}}f)
then
{\mathscr{K}}\!f\supseteq\{f\}+\mathfrak{a}^{3}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.

Note that
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}\supseteq Fitt_{0}(T^{1}_{\mathscr{R}}f)
, and for
p\geq 2
one often has
{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}\supseteq Fitt_{0}(T^{1}_{\mathscr{R}}f)
, see example

3.14
.

ii.

If
(f)\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}^{3}
then
{\mathscr{K}}\!f\supseteq\{f\}+(\mathfrak{a}^{2}_{\mathscr{R}}+{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot(f))\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.
For
p=1
this reads:
{\mathscr{K}}\!f\supseteq\{f\}+Jac(f)^{2}+f\cdot{\mathfrak{m}}
.

iii.

For
p=1
and
f\in{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}
use Morse lemma, example
4.4
.ii, to present
f=Q_{2}(x)+{\tilde{f}}(w)
, with
{\tilde{f}}(w)\in(w)^{3}
.
Then we get:
{\mathscr{K}}\!f\supseteq\{f\}+{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot(x)\cdot((x)+Jac_{w}({\tilde{f}}(w)))+(Jac_{w}({\tilde{f}}(w)))^{2}+{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot(f)
.

iv.

For
p\geq 2
the assumption on the modules
\mathfrak{a}^{2}\cdot I^{ord(f)-2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot\mathfrak{a}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f+{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot(f)\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
is
weaker than the assumption on the ideals
\mathfrak{a}^{2}\cdot I^{ord(f)-2}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot\mathfrak{a}\cdot\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}+{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot(f).

But the later assumption is often simpler to verify.

v.

Below we assume
p=1
.

•

Suppose
\mathfrak{a}^{2}\cdot{\mathfrak{m}}^{ord(f)-2}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot\mathfrak{a}\cdot Jac(f)+f\cdot{\mathfrak{m}}
. Then
{\mathscr{K}}\!f\supseteq\{f\}+\mathfrak{a}\cdot Jac(f)+f\cdot{\mathfrak{m}}

•

Suppose
{\mathfrak{m}}^{e}\cdot Jac(f)+(f){\mathfrak{m}}\supseteq{\mathfrak{m}}^{d}
for
e=\lceil\frac{d-ord(f)}{2}\rceil+1
.

Take
\mathfrak{a}={\mathfrak{m}}^{e}
to get:

{\mathscr{K}}\!f\supset\{f\}+({\mathfrak{m}}^{e}\cdot Jac(f)+{\mathfrak{m}}(f))\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.

•

Take the smallest
d\in\mathbb{N}
satisfying
{\mathfrak{m}}^{2d+ord(f)-2}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}^{d+1}\cdot Jac(f)+{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot(f)
.

Then
{\mathscr{K}}\!f\supseteq\{f\}+{\mathfrak{m}}^{d}\cdot Jac(f)+{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot f
.

5.2.
An application: the
{\mathscr{K}}\!
-orbit of a reduced complete intersection curve germ via the semigroup of values

Let
R_{X}
be one of
\Bbbk[[x]]
,
\Bbbk\{x\}
,
\Bbbk\langle x\rangle
, where
\Bbbk=\bar{\Bbbk}
is a field, see §
2.1
.ii.
Suppose
(C,o):=V(f)\subset(\Bbbk^{n},o)
is a reduced complete intersection curve germ. (Here
f=(f_{1},\dots,f_{n-1})
.)
Take its branch decomposition,
(C,o)=\cup^{r}_{i=1}(C_{i},o)
. We have the normalization morphism

\mathcal{O}_{(C,o)}{\hookrightarrow}\prod\mathcal{O}_{(\tilde{C}_{i},o)}
. Here each ring
\mathcal{O}_{(\tilde{C}_{i},o)}
is a DVR.
It has the valuation map
val_{i}:\mathcal{O}_{(\tilde{C}_{i},o)}\ \lower 1.72218pt\hbox{$\mathrel{\mathop{\to}\limits}$}\ {\bar{\mathbb{N}}}:=\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
.
The “full” valuation map is the product of morphisms,
val:R_{X}\to\mathcal{O}_{(C,o)}{\hookrightarrow}\prod\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{C}_{i},o}\to\oplus_{i}{\bar{\mathbb{N}}}
.
Accordingly we have two sub-semigroups:
val(\mathcal{O}_{(C,o)})\subseteq\oplus_{i}{\bar{\mathbb{N}}}
and

val(\mathfrak{a}^{2}_{\mathscr{R}})\subseteq\oplus_{i}{\bar{\mathbb{N}}}
. Note that
val(\mathfrak{a}^{2}_{\mathscr{R}})=val(\mathfrak{a}^{2}_{\mathscr{R}}+(f))
.

Below we consider the “
\mathbb{N}
-submodules” of
\mathbb{N}^{r}
, i.e. subsets
S\subset\mathbb{N}^{r}
satisfying:
S+\mathbb{N}^{r}=S
.

Proposition 5.3
.

Suppose a submodule
{\mathcal{S}}\subset{\bar{\mathbb{N}}}^{r}
satisfies:
{\mathcal{S}}\cap val(R_{X})={\mathcal{S}}\cap val({\mathfrak{m}}\cdot\mathfrak{a}^{2}_{\mathscr{R}})
.
Then
{\mathscr{K}}\!f\supseteq\{f\}+{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot val^{-1}({\mathcal{S}})\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.

If
(C,o)
is not a plane curve germ, i.e.
p\geq 2
, then
in many cases one has
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f,
rather than just
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq T_{\mathscr{R}}f,
see example
3.14
.iv.
Then the bound is strengthened to:
{\mathscr{K}}\!f\supseteq\{f\}+val^{-1}({\mathcal{S}})\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.

Proof.
Let
g\in val^{-1}({\mathcal{S}})\subset R_{X}
. Then
val(g)=val(q)
for an element
q\in{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot\mathfrak{a}^{2}_{\mathscr{R}}
. Thus
val(g-c\cdot q)>val(g)

for some constant
c\in\Bbbk
.
Namely,
val_{i}(g-c\cdot q)\geq val_{i}(g)
for each
i=1,\dots,r
, and this inequality is strict for at least one
i
.

Apply this procedure for each coordinate of the vector
val(g)\in{\bar{\mathbb{N}}}^{r}
to get an element
q\in\mathfrak{a}^{2}_{\mathscr{R}}
that satisfies:

val(g-c\cdot q)\geq val(g)+(1,\dots,1)
.
Iterating this we get
val^{-1}({\mathcal{S}})\subset\mathfrak{a}^{2}_{\mathscr{R}}+(f)+val^{-1}({\mathcal{S}}+d(1,\dots,1))
for each
d\geq 1
.

As
(C,o)
is a reduced germ, the ideal
\mathfrak{a}^{2}_{\mathscr{R}}+(f)
is
{\mathfrak{m}}
-primary. Therefore we get:
val^{-1}({\mathcal{S}})\subset{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot\mathfrak{a}^{2}_{\mathscr{R}}+(f)
.
And hence
\{f\}+{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot val^{-1}({\mathcal{S}})\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subset\{f\}+{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot({\mathfrak{m}}\cdot\mathfrak{a}^{2}_{\mathscr{R}}+(f))\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.
Finally, apply Example
5.2
.i.

\blacksquare

5.3.
The filtration criterion for
{\mathscr{K}}\!
-orbits

Take the filtration ​
M_{\bullet}\!\!:=\!I^{\bullet}\!\cdot\!R^{\oplus p}_{X}.
Fix some integers
1\!\leq\!j\!<\!d.

Theorem 5.4
.

Suppose the conditions
jet_{N}(Exp)
,
jet_{N}(Ln)
of §
2.5

hold for
N=\lceil\frac{d-ord(f)}{j}\rceil
. Then:

{{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(j)}f}\supseteq\{f\}+I^{d}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}

if and only if

{T_{{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(j)}}f}\supseteq I^{d}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.

Proof.

Step 1.

In the proof of
\mathscr{R}
-case, Theorem
4.5
,
we have used the transitions
T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}}\rightleftarrows\mathscr{R}^{(j)}
, ensured by the
jet_{N}
assumptions.
Now we need the transitions
T_{{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(j)}}\rightleftarrows{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(j)}
.
These are ensured by lemma 3.22 of
[
B.G.K.22
]
:

\bullet

For any element
(u,\xi)\in T_{{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(j)}}
there exists an element
(U,\Phi)\in{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(j)}
satisfying:

ord_{I}[(U,\Phi)f-f-(u,\xi)f]>ord_{I}[(u,\xi)f].

\bullet

For any element
(U,\Phi)\in{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(j)}
there exists an element
(u,\xi)\in T_{{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(j)}}
satisfying:

ord_{I}[(U,\Phi)f-f-(u,\xi)f]>ord_{I}[(U,\Phi)f-f].

The statement of that lemma in
[
B.G.K.22
]
assumes the conditions
jet_{N}(Exp)
,
jet_{N}(Ln)
for each
N
.
In particular this implies:
\Bbbk\supseteq\mathbb{Q}
.
However, the proof in
[
B.G.K.22
]
uses these conditions only up to
N=\lceil\frac{d-ord(f)}{j}\rceil
, as we explain now.

The only part of the proof involving the conditions
jet_{N}(Exp)
,
jet_{N}(Ln)
is the Baker-Campbell-Hausdorff formula,
§
2.3
.iv.
For the transitions
T_{{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(j)}}\rightleftarrows{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(j)}
we need the expansion only up to order
l
that satisfies:
l\cdot j+ord(f)\geq d
.
Thus it suffices to expand up to order
l=\lceil\frac{d-ord(f)}{j}\rceil
, which
coincides with
N
. Our
jet_{N}(\dots)
assumptions imply
2,\dots,N\in\Bbbk^{\times}
. Therefore the factor
(l!)^{4}
is invertible.
Therefore the Baker-Campbell-Hausdorff formula, (
5
),
holds in
R_{X}
up to order
l=N
. Thus lemma 3.22 of
[
B.G.K.22
]
is applicable to our case.

Step 2.

We restate lemma 3.22 of
[
B.G.K.22
]
in the form:

(40)

w_{d}\in T_{{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(j)}}f+M_{d+1}\quad\quad\text{ iff }\quad\quad w_{d}\in({\mathscr{K}}\!^{(j)}f-f)+M_{d+1}.

Using this equivalence the proof goes as follows.

•

If ​
\overline{{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(j)}}f\!\supseteq\!\{f\}\!+\!M_{d}
then ​
T_{{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(j)}}f\!+\!M_{d+1}\!\supseteq\!M_{d}
. As ​
M_{d+1}\!=\!I\!\cdot\!M_{d}
we get (by Nakayama):
​
T_{{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(j)}}f\!\supseteq\!M_{d}.

•

If
T_{{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(j)}}f\supseteq M_{d}
then
T_{{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(j+l)}}f\supseteq M_{d+l}
for every
l\geq 0
.
For each
l
we use (
40
) with
N=\frac{d+l-ord(f)}{j+l}\leq\frac{d-ord(f)}{j}
.
And then

{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(j+l)}f+M_{d+l+1}\supseteq\{f\}+M_{d+l}
for every
l\geq 0
. Thus
\overline{{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(j)}}f\supseteq\{f\}+M_{d}.

Finally, apply part 2 of Lemma
3.6
.

\blacksquare

Corollary 5.5
.

(
R_{X}
​is ​one ​of
\Bbbk[[x]],{\Bbbk\{x\}},{\Bbbk\langle x\rangle}{}
, ​with ​
\Bbbk
a ​local ​ring, ​see​ §
2.1
.ii.)
Take
I\!\!\subseteq\!\!(x)
and
f\!\in\!(x)\!R^{\oplus p}_{X}.

1.​​

Suppose
2,3,\!\dots,\!N\!\in\!\Bbbk^{\times}
for
N\!=\!\lceil\frac{d-ord(f)}{j}\rceil.

Then:

{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(j)}f\!\supseteq\!\{f\}\!+I^{d}\!\cdot\!R^{\oplus p}_{X}
if and only if

{T_{{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(j)}}f}\!\supseteq\!I^{d}\!\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}.

2.​​

Suppose
2\in\Bbbk^{\times}.
If
T_{{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(j)}}f\supseteq I^{d}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}

then
{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(d-j-ord(f))}f\supseteq\{f\}+I^{2d-2j-ord(f)}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.

The proofs of both parts are the same as in Corollary
4.6
.

Example 5.6
.

i.

Take
p\!=\!1
,
I\!=\!{\mathfrak{m}}
,
j\!=\!1
and suppose
\Bbbk
is a field,
char(\Bbbk)\!=\!0
or
char(\Bbbk)\!>\!d-ord(f).

(41)

Then:
{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(1)}f\supseteq\{f\}+{\mathfrak{m}}^{d}
if and only if
{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\cdot Jac(f)+{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot(f)\supseteq{\mathfrak{m}}^{d}
.

In particular, the
{\mathscr{K}}\!
-order of determinacy of
f
is
\leq\tau(f)+1.

For
char(\Bbbk)=0
this is well known, e.g.
[
Gr.Lo.Sh
, Corollary 2.24]
.
For
char(\Bbbk)>d-ord(f)
this strengthens the bound
(2\tau(f)-ord(f)+2)
of
[
Bou.Gr.Ma.11
]
,
[
Greuel-Pham.19
]
.

ii.

For
j=1
(and
\Bbbk
a field) part two of Corollary
5.5
is known, e.g. see Corollary 5.10 of
[
B.G.K.22
]
.

6.
Properties of the (extended) tangent space
T_{\mathscr{A}}f

The tangent space
T_{\mathscr{A}}f
is not an
R_{X}
-module. And
T_{\mathscr{A}}f
is not finitely generated as a module over
R_{Y}
.
As is mentioned in §
1.6
, numerous simple/immediate properties of
T_{\mathscr{R}}f,T_{\mathscr{K}}\!f
become cumbersome (and non-trivial) for
T_{\mathscr{A}}f
.
E.g. even the simplest properties of
T_{\mathscr{A}}f
in the classical
C^{\infty}
-case rely heavily on the deep Mather-Malgrange preparation theorem.

Below we study the annihilator
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}
,
the extended module structure of
T_{\mathscr{A}}f
, the Nakayama&Artin-Rees properties, and the topological closure
\overline{T_{\mathscr{A}}f}\subseteq R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.

Let the pair
(R_{X},R_{Y})
be one of

({\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk[[x]]$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$J$}\right.},\Bbbk[[y]])
,
({\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk\{x\}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$J$}\right.},\Bbbk\{y\})
,
({\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk\langle x\rangle$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$J$}\right.},\Bbbk\langle y\rangle)
, §
2.1
.ii.
Fix the filtration
I^{\bullet}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
of
R^{\oplus p}_{X},
we always assume
(f)\subseteq I
.
Accordingly one has the filtrations
\mathscr{A}^{(\bullet)}
and
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(\bullet)}}
, with
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(-1)}}:=T_{\mathscr{A}}
,
see §
3.3.2
. Fix a map
f:X\to(\Bbbk^{p},o)
, i.e.
f\in{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.
Take the annihilator ideals
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}},\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}\subset R_{X}
defining the instability locus, see §
3.6
.
In general
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}},\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}\not\subseteq f^{\#}(R_{Y})
.

The map
f
is called infinitesimally
\mathscr{A}
-stable if
T_{\mathscr{A}}f=R^{\oplus p}_{X}
, i.e.
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}=R_{X}
.

6.1.
The annihilator
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}

6.1.1.

The case
p=1,
i.e.
f:X\to(\Bbbk^{1},o),
is elementary. (Probably well-known.)

Lemma 6.1
.

(
p=1
)
Assuming
f\in{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}
, one has
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}\subseteq\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\subseteq\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}:{\mathfrak{m}}
and
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}=\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cap f^{\#}(R_{Y})
.

In particular, for
R_{X}
one of
\Bbbk[[x]]
,
\Bbbk\{x\}
,
\Bbbk\langle x\rangle
, one has:
Jac(f)\subseteq\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\subseteq Jac(f):{\mathfrak{m}}
.

Proof.
(The part
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}\subseteq\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}
is obvious.)
Take any
g\in\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}
, then
g\in\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}+f^{\#}(y)^{d}
.
For any
q\in{\mathfrak{m}}
one has
q\cdot g\in\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}+f^{\#}(y)^{d^{\prime}}
. (Here we chose
d,d^{\prime}\leq\infty
as the largest possible.)

•

If
d^{\prime}\leq d
then
(1-\tilde{q}\cdot q)g\in\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}
for some
\tilde{q}\in R_{Y}\subset R_{X}
. And therefore
g\in\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}
.

•

Suppose
d^{\prime}\!>\!d
for each
q\!\in\!{\mathfrak{m}}
. Then
{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot g\subseteq\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}+g\cdot(f)\!\subseteq\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}+g\cdot{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}
.
Nakayama gives:
{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot g\subseteq\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}
.

In both cases we get
{\mathfrak{m}}\!\cdot\!\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\!\subseteq\!\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}
. Hence
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\!\subseteq\!\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}\!:\!{\mathfrak{m}}
.
The statement
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}=\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cap f^{\#}(R_{Y})
follows.
\blacksquare

6.1.2.

​​​For ​
p\!\geq\!2
the annihilator ​
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}
is much more complicated. ​We list several basic properties.
​Most of them

are immediate/well known. ​Let the pair ​
(R_{X},R_{Y})
be as in ​§
2.2
.
​Take the filtration ​
I^{\bullet}\!\cdot\!R^{\oplus p}_{X},
​with
(f)\!\subseteq\!I.

Properties 6.2
.

i.

\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{K}}\!\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}+\Bbbk^{\oplus p}
.

ii.

If
(f)\subseteq\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}
then
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{K}}\!\subseteq\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}
.

If
f
is infinitesimally stable, i.e.
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}=R_{X}
, then
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(0)}}f=T_{{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(0)}}f
.

(In Lemma 7.1 of
[
Mon. Nuñ.-Bal.
]
this is stated for
I={\mathfrak{m}}
,
but the proof is the same.)

More generally,
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(0)}}f=T_{{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(0)}}f
iff
(f)\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq T_{\mathscr{A}^{(0)}}f
iff
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(0)}}=\mathfrak{a}_{{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(0)}}
.

iii.

If
(f)\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}
and
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}
then
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}+(f)\subseteq\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{K}}\!
.

iv.

If
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}
then
\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}+(f)}=\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{K}}\!}\subset R_{X}
.
Moreover,
\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}+(f)}=\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(j)}}}
for
j\geq 0
.

(Geometrically: if
f
is
\mathscr{A}
-unstable then the singular locus
Sing(f)
coincides with the instability locus restricted to the germ
V(f)
.)

Proof.
The part
\mathbf{\subseteq}
.
For
d\!\gg\!1
we have:

(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}+(f))^{d}\!\cdot\!R^{\oplus p}_{X}\!\subseteq\!T_{\mathscr{R}}f+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(0)}}f\!\subseteq\!T_{\mathscr{R}}f+(f)\!\cdot\!R^{\oplus p}_{X}=T_{\mathscr{K}}\!f.

The case
j\geq 0
is immediate.

The part
\mathbf{\supseteq}
.
Take the filtration
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\bullet}\subset R_{X}
.
Then
\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}+(f)}\supseteq\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(0)}}+(f)}\supseteq\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}^{(0)}}+(f)}=\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}+(f)}=\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{K}}\!}
.
The last transition is by example
3.14
.

The statement with
j\geq 0
is similar:

\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}+(f)}\supseteq\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}}+(f)}=\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(j)}}}
.

\blacksquare

v.

If
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{K}}\!\subseteq\sqrt{I}
and
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}
then
\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}+(f)}=\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}+(f)}
for each
j\geq 0
.

Proof.
\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}+(f)}=\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(j)}}}\supseteq\sqrt{I\cdot\mathfrak{a}_{{\mathscr{K}}\!}}=\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{K}}\!}=\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}+(f)}
.

\blacksquare

vi.

\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cdot\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{K}}\!\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}+(f))\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(0)}}f
.

If
(f)\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}
and
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}
then
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cdot\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{K}}\!\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}+(f))\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f
.

vii.

\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(0)}}\cdot\mathfrak{a}_{{\mathscr{K}}\!}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(0)}}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f+(f)\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}^{(0)}}f+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f
.

\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(0)}}\cdot\mathfrak{a}_{{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(0)}}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(0)}}+(f))\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}^{(0)}}f+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f
.

\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cdot\mathfrak{a}^{2}_{\mathscr{K}}\!\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cdot\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{K}}\!+(f)^{2})\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f
.

viii.

\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}}=\sqrt{I\cdot\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}}
for every
j\geq 0
.

Proof.
The part
\subseteq
.
By the assumption
(f)\subseteq I
. Therefore
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f\subseteq I\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
. Thus
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}\subseteq I\cap\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}
.
Therefore (by Artin-Rees or via the primary decomposition)
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}\subseteq\sqrt{I\cdot\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}}
.

The part
\supseteq
.
The quotient
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$T_{\mathscr{A}}f$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$T_{\mathscr{R}}f$}\right.}
is a finitely-generated module over
R_{Y}
.
Thus the submodule
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}+T_{\mathscr{R}}f$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$T_{\mathscr{R}}f$}\right.}\subseteq{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$T_{\mathscr{A}}f$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$T_{\mathscr{R}}f$}\right.}
is finitely generated,
see §
2.1
.vi. Take the filtration
(I\cdot\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}})^{\bullet}\cdot{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}+T_{\mathscr{R}}f$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$T_{\mathscr{R}}f$}\right.}
.
It is strictly decreasing.
Therefore for each
i\geq 1
there exists
d_{i}<\infty
satisfying:

(I\cdot\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}})^{d_{i}}\cdot{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{4.13332pt}{$\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}+T_{\mathscr{R}}f$}\!\!\diagup\!\!\raisebox{-4.13332pt}{$T_{\mathscr{R}}f$}\right.}\subset f^{\#}(y)^{i+1}\cdot{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{4.13332pt}{$T_{\mathscr{A}}f$}\!\!\diagup\!\!\raisebox{-4.13332pt}{$T_{\mathscr{R}}f$}\right.}.

Hence
(I\cdot\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}})^{d_{i}+1}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subset T_{\mathscr{R}}f+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(i)}}f
. Then

(I\cdot\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}})^{d_{i}+1}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subset T_{\mathscr{R}}f\cap I^{i+1}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(i)}}f
, because

(f)\subseteq I
. Artin-Rees over
R_{X}
gives:

(I\cdot\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}})^{d}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subset T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}}f+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(j)}}f
for each
j\geq 0
and
d\gg j
. Hence

\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}}\supseteq I\cdot\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}
.

\blacksquare

6.1.3.

The following technical lemma is used to apply theorem
7.4
in particular cases.

Lemma 6.3
.

​1.​​

(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cap f^{\#}(y))^{2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cap f^{\#}(y))\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f+(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cap f^{\#}(y))\cdot T_{\mathscr{L}}f
.

​2.​​

(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}^{(0)}}+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(0)}}\cap f^{\#}(y))^{2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}^{(0)}}+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(0)}}\cap f^{\#}(y))\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}^{(0)}}f+(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(0)}}\cap f^{\#}(y))\cdot T_{\mathscr{L}^{(0)}}f
.

Proof.
(of the second statement) Take the largest
R_{Y}
-submodule
M\subseteq T_{\mathscr{A}^{(0)}}f
such that
M\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
is an
R_{X}
-submodule.
(Thus
M
is uniquely defined.) In particular
M\supseteq T_{\mathscr{R}^{(0)}}f+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(0)}}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.
Therefore we can present
M=T_{\mathscr{R}^{(0)}}f+\Lambda
for some
R_{Y}
-submodule
\Lambda\subseteq T_{\mathscr{L}^{(0)}}f
. We take
\Lambda
the largest possible,
thus
\Lambda\supseteq\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(0)}}R^{\oplus p}_{X}\cap T_{\mathscr{L}^{(0)}}f.
Therefore
Ann({\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$R^{\oplus p}_{Y}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$\Lambda$}\right.})\supseteq\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(0)}}\cap f^{\#}(y).

We claim:
Ann({\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$R^{\oplus p}_{Y}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$\Lambda$}\right.})=\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(0)}}\cap f^{\#}(y)
. Indeed, suppose
\Lambda\supseteq\mathfrak{b}_{Y}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{Y}

for an ideal
\mathfrak{b}_{Y}\subset f^{\#}(R_{Y})
. Then
M\supseteq R_{X}\cdot(\mathfrak{b}_{Y}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{Y})
. And thus
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(0)}}\supseteq R_{X}\cdot\mathfrak{b}_{y}
.
Hence
\mathfrak{b}_{Y}\subseteq\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(0)}}\cap f^{\#}(R_{Y})
.

Altogether:
(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(0)}}\cap f^{\#}(y))\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq M=T_{\mathscr{R}^{(0)}}f+\Lambda
. Therefore

(42)

(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}^{(0)}}+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(0)}}\cap f^{\#}(y))^{2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}=(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}^{(0)}}+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(0)}}\cap f^{\#}(y))\cdot\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}^{(0)}}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}+(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(0)}}\cap f^{\#}(y))^{2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq

\hskip 227.62204pt\subseteq(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}^{(0)}}+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(0)}}\cap f^{\#}(y))\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}^{(0)}}+(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(0)}}\cap f^{\#}(y))\cdot\Lambda.\hskip 28.45274pt{\hfill\blacksquare}

6.2.
Extending the module structure of
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(\bullet)}}f

The tangent spaces
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(\bullet)}}f\subset R^{\oplus p}_{X}
are
R_{Y}
-submodules. In fact they are modules over a larger ring, and are often finitely generated.

Lemma 6.4
.

1.

\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cdot T_{\mathscr{A}}f\subseteq T_{\mathscr{A}}f
.

2.

\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cdot T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f\subseteq T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f
for each
j\geq 0
.

Therefore all the spaces
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f
are modules over the subring
f^{\#}(R_{Y})+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\subseteq R_{X}
.

3.

The map
f
is of finite singularity type (i.e. the
\Bbbk
-module
T^{1}_{\mathscr{K}}\!f
is finitely generated, §
3.6.2
)
if and only if the
\Bbbk
-module
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$R_{X}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$(f)+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}$}\right.}
is finitely generated.

4.

If
f
is of finite singularity type, then any
(f^{\#}(R_{Y})+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}})
-submodule of
R^{\oplus p}_{X}
is finitely generated.

In particular,
R^{\oplus p}_{X}
and
T_{\mathscr{A}}f
are f.g. over
the local Noetherian ring
f^{\#}(R_{Y})+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}
.

Proof.

1.

Immediate verification:
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cdot T_{\mathscr{A}}f\subseteq\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq T_{\mathscr{A}}f
.

2.

We have:

\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cdot T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f\ \lower 1.72218pt\hbox{$\mathrel{\mathop{=}\limits^{\lx@sectionsign\ref{Sec.3.Filtrations.on.G.TG}}}$}\ \mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\big(T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}}f+f^{\#}(\mathfrak{b}_{j})\cdot T_{\mathscr{L}}f\big)\subseteq T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}}f+f^{\#}(\mathfrak{b}_{j})\cdot T_{\mathscr{A}}f
. Note that
f^{\#}(\mathfrak{b}_{j})\subseteq I^{j+1}
, as
(f)\!\subseteq\!I
.
Thus ​
f^{\#}(\mathfrak{b}_{j})\!\cdot\!T_{\mathscr{R}}f\!\subseteq\!T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}}f.

In addition ​
f^{\#}(\mathfrak{b}_{j})\!\cdot\!T_{\mathscr{L}}f\!\subseteq\!T_{\mathscr{L}^{(j)}}f.

Therefore
f^{\#}(\mathfrak{b}_{j})\!\cdot\!T_{\mathscr{A}}f\!\subseteq\!T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f.

3.

The subring
f^{\#}(R_{Y})+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\subseteq R_{X}
is local as the rings
R_{Y}
,
R_{X}
are local and
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\subseteq R_{X}
is an ideal.

We can assume
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}
. Then, by property
6.2
.iv we have
\sqrt{(f)+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}}=\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{K}}\!}
.
Hence
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$R_{X}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$(f)+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}$}\right.}
is
\Bbbk
-finite iff
\sqrt{(f)+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}}\supseteq{\mathfrak{m}}
iff
T^{1}_{\mathscr{K}}\!f
is
\Bbbk
-finite.

4.

By part 3:
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$R_{X}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}+(f)$}\right.}
is finitely generated over
\Bbbk
.

Applying Weierstraß finiteness to
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$R_{X}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}$}\right.}\otimes{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$R_{Y}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$(y)$}\right.}={\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$R_{X}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}+(f)$}\right.}
we get:
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$R_{X}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}$}\right.}
is f.g. over
R_{Y}
.
And then
R_{X}
is a f.g. module over
f^{\#}(R_{Y})+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}
.
(Fix a finite set of
R_{X}
-preimages of the generators of
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$R_{X}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}$}\right.}\in mod\text{-}f^{\#}(R_{Y})
. They generate
R_{X}
over
f^{\#}(R_{Y})+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}
.)

Therefore the subring
f^{\#}(R_{Y})+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\subset R_{X}
is Noetherian. (This is the Eakin-Nagata theorem,
[
Eisenbud
, Exercise A.3.7, pg.625]
.)

Finally, apply the remark of §
2.1
.viii to
R^{\oplus p}_{X}
as a f.g. module over
f^{\#}(R_{Y})+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}
.

\blacksquare

6.3.
Nakayama-type results for
T_{\mathscr{A}}f

Suppose a submodule
M\subseteq R^{\oplus p}_{X}
satisfies
T_{\mathscr{G}}f+{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot M\supseteq M
. For
\mathscr{G}=\mathscr{R},{\mathscr{K}}\!
one
concludes (by Nakayama):
T_{\mathscr{G}}f\supseteq M
. But
T_{\mathscr{A}}f
is not an
R_{X}
-module.
If considered as
R_{Y}
-modules,
T_{\mathscr{A}}f,M

are not always finitely generated. Recall the “adjusted Nakayama lemma”.

Lemma 6.5
.

[
Gaffney.79
]
,
[
Wall.81
, ​lemma ​1.6]

Take a homomorphism of (local, Noetherian) rings
f\!:\!\!(R_{Y},{\mathfrak{m}}_{Y})\!\!\to\!\!(R_{X},{\mathfrak{m}})
,
with Weierstraß finiteness condition. ​​Take f.g. ​​modules
M_{X}\!\!\in\!mod\text{-}R_{X}
,
M_{Y}\!\!\in\!mod\text{-}R_{Y}.

​1.​​

If
M_{X}\subseteq M_{Y}+(f)\cdot M_{X}
then
M_{X}\subseteq M_{Y}
.

​2.​​

Suppose the inclusion
M_{X}\subseteq M_{Y}+\mathfrak{b}\cdot M_{X}
holds for an ideal
\mathfrak{b}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}
satisfying:

\mathfrak{b}\cdot M_{Y}\subseteq(f)\cdot M_{X}
.
Then
M_{X}\subseteq M_{Y}
.

Proof.

​1.​​

One has
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$R_{Y}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$(y)$}\right.}\otimes M_{X}\cong{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$M_{X}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$(f)M_{X}$}\right.}\subseteq{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$M_{Y}+(f)M_{X}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$(f)M_{X}$}\right.}
. Here
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$M_{Y}+(f)M_{X}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$(f)M_{X}$}\right.}
is f.g. over
R_{Y}
.
Thus
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$R_{Y}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$(y)$}\right.}\otimes M_{X}
is f.g. over
R_{Y}
, see §
2.1
.vi.
By Weierstraß finiteness
M_{X}
is f.g. over
R_{Y}
. Finally, apply Nakayama over
R_{Y}
to the inclusion
M_{X}\subseteq M_{Y}+(f)\cdot M_{X}
.

​2.​​

One gets
\mathfrak{b}\cdot M_{X}\subseteq(f)\cdot M_{X}+\mathfrak{b}^{2}\cdot M_{X}
. Nakayama (over
R_{X}
) gives

\mathfrak{b}\cdot M_{X}\!\subseteq\!(f)\cdot M_{X}.
The initial assumption gives
M_{X}\!\subseteq\!M_{Y}\!+\!\!(f)\!\cdot\!M_{X}
. Apply part one.

\blacksquare

Corollary 6.6
.

Fix some
j\geq-1
, and
M\subseteq R^{\oplus p}_{X}
, and
(f)\subseteq I
.

1.

If
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f+(f)\cdot M\supseteq M
then
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f\supseteq M
.

2.

Suppose
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}
. If
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f+(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}+(f))\cdot M\supseteq M
then
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f\supseteq M
.

Proof.
As we need an upper bound on
M
, we can assume
M\supseteq T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}}f
.

1.

Pass to the quotients,
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}}f$}\right.}+(f)\cdot{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$M$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}}f$}\right.}\supseteq{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$M$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}}f$}\right.}
.
Here
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$M$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}}f$}\right.}\in mod\text{-}R_{X}
and
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}}f$}\right.}\in mod\text{-}R_{Y}
.
Therefore (by part one of lemma
6.5
)
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}}f$}\right.}\supseteq{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$M$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}}f$}\right.}
.

2.

Multiply the assumption by
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}
to get
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cdot T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cdot(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}+(f))\cdot M\!\supseteq\!\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\!\cdot\!M.

Nakayama over
R_{X}
gives

\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\!\cdot\!T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f\!\supseteq\!\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\!\cdot\!M.

Part two of lemma
6.4
gives:
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f\!\supseteq\!\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\!\cdot\!M.

The initial assumption becomes:
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f\!+\!(f)\!\cdot\!M\!\supseteq\!M.

Now invoke part 1.

\blacksquare

Example 6.7
.

Let
(R_{X},R_{Y})
be one of the pairs
({\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk[[x]]$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$J$}\right.},\Bbbk[[y]])
,
({\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk\{x\}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$J$}\right.},\Bbbk\{y\})
,
({\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk\langle x\rangle$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$J$}\right.},\Bbbk\langle y\rangle)
,
see §
2.1
.ii.

Let
I={\mathfrak{m}}
and suppose
{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}
. Take
M={\mathfrak{m}}^{j}\cdot(f)\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
for some
j\geq 0
.
Part 2 of corollary
6.6
gives:

(43)

If
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f+(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}+(f))\cdot{\mathfrak{m}}^{j}\cdot(f)\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\supseteq{\mathfrak{m}}^{j}(f)R^{\oplus p}_{X}
then

T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f=T_{{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(j)}}f
.

For
j=0
this is well known, e.g. theorem 5.1 of
[
Ruas.83
]
.

Remark 6.8
.

One would like a stronger bound:
​“If ​
T_{\mathscr{A}}f\!+\!(\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}}\!+\!(f))\!\cdot\!M\!\!\supseteq\!\!M
then
T_{\mathscr{A}}f\!\!\supseteq\!\!M
”. This fails already for the case ​
\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}}\!=\!{\mathfrak{m}}.

For example, one always has
T_{\mathscr{A}}f\!+\!{\mathfrak{m}}\!\cdot\!R^{\oplus p}_{X}\!=\!R^{\oplus p}_{X},
​ though of course
T_{\mathscr{A}}f\!\neq\!R^{\oplus p}_{X}.

Corollary 6.9
.

Suppose
\mathfrak{b}\!\cdot\!R^{\oplus p}_{X}\!\subseteq\!T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f+\mathfrak{b}\cdot(\mathfrak{b}(f):(f)^{j+1})R^{\oplus p}_{X}
, for an ideal
\mathfrak{b}\subset R_{X}
.
Then
\mathfrak{b}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\!\subseteq\!T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f.

Proof.
Pass to the quotients,
\mathfrak{b}\!\cdot\!{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$R^{\oplus p}_{X}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}}f$}\right.}\!\subseteq\!{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}}f$}\right.}+\mathfrak{b}\!\cdot\!(\mathfrak{b}(f):(f)^{j+1})\!\cdot\!{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$R^{\oplus p}_{X}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}}f$}\right.}.

Note:
(\mathfrak{b}(f):(f)^{j+1})\!\cdot\!{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}}f$}\right.}\!\subseteq\!\mathfrak{b}\!\cdot\!(f)\!\cdot\!{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$R^{\oplus p}_{X}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}}f$}\right.}.

Then (by part 2 of lemma
6.5
):
\mathfrak{b}\!\cdot\!{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$R^{\oplus p}_{X}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}}f$}\right.}\!\subseteq\!{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}}f$}\right.}.

\blacksquare

Example 6.10
.

i.

Take
\mathfrak{b}=I^{r}
and
(f)\subseteq I,
for some
0\leq j<r.
One gets the well-known bound: if

T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f+I^{2r-j}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\supseteq I^{r}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
then
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f\supseteq I^{r}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.  (See e.g. corollary 1.23, pg. 17 of
[
Ruas.83
]
.)

ii.

Moreover, for any map
g\in{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
satisfying
g-f\in I^{2r-2j}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}

one gets (by the direct check)
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}g\supseteq I^{r}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
. For
I={\mathfrak{m}}
this is
[
du Plessis.80
, corollary 2.4]
.

6.4.
Artin-Rees type properties of
T_{\mathscr{A}}f

The image tangent spaces
T_{\mathscr{R}}f
,
T_{\mathscr{K}}\!f\subseteq R^{\oplus p}_{X}
are
R_{X}
-submodules. For them the condition
“
T_{\mathscr{G}^{(j)}}f\supseteq I^{d}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
” implies “
T_{\mathscr{G}^{(j+\bullet)}}f\supseteq I^{d+\bullet}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
”, for every
\bullet\geq 0
.
This is heavily used in the study of
\mathscr{R}
,
{\mathscr{K}}\!
-orbits, theorems
4.5
,
5.4
.

The tangent space
T_{\mathscr{A}}f
is a module over
R_{Y}
, not over
R_{X}
.
The condition
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(\bullet)}}f\supseteq I^{d}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
does not imply “
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j+\bullet)}}f\supseteq I^{d+\bullet}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
for every
\bullet\geq 0
”.
(For example, in the infinitesimally stable case, i.e.
T_{\mathscr{A}}f=R^{\oplus p}_{X}
, one does not have
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(0)}}f\supseteq{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.)
Yet, some properties of Artin-Rees type do hold.

Take a map
X\!\ \lower 1.72218pt\hbox{$\mathrel{\mathop{\to}\limits^{f}}$}\ \!Y=(\Bbbk^{p},o),
the corresponding map
R_{Y}\!\ \lower 1.72218pt\hbox{$\mathrel{\mathop{\to}\limits^{f^{\#}}}$}\ \!R_{X},
and a pair
mod\text{-}R_{Y}\ni\!M_{Y}\!\subset\!M_{X}\!\in\!mod\text{-}R_{X}.

Lemma 6.11
.

Then
M_{Y}\cap{\mathfrak{m}}^{d_{j}}\cdot M_{X}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}^{j}_{Y}\cdot M_{Y}\cap{\mathfrak{m}}^{d_{j}}_{X}\cdot M_{X}
for every
j

and a corresponding
d_{j}<\infty
.

Proof.
The filtration
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\bullet}_{X}\cdot M_{X}
is strictly decreasing (by Nakayama over
R_{X}
). Take the induced filtration

{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$M_{Y}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{${\mathfrak{m}}^{j}_{Y}\cdot M_{Y}$}\right.}\supset{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$M_{Y}\cap{\mathfrak{m}}^{\bullet}_{X}\cdot M_{X}+{\mathfrak{m}}^{j}_{Y}\cdot M_{Y}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{${\mathfrak{m}}^{j}_{Y}\cdot M_{Y}$}\right.}
. Here

{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$M_{Y}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{${\mathfrak{m}}^{j}_{Y}\cdot M_{Y}$}\right.}
is an Artinian module over the ring
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$R_{Y}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{${\mathfrak{m}}^{j}_{Y}$}\right.}
. Thus the induced filtration stabilizes,

(44)

{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{4.13332pt}{$M_{Y}\cap{\mathfrak{m}}^{d}_{X}\cdot M_{X}+{\mathfrak{m}}^{j}_{Y}\cdot M_{Y}$}\!\!\diagup\!\!\raisebox{-4.13332pt}{${\mathfrak{m}}^{j}_{Y}\cdot M_{Y}$}\right.}=\cap_{\bullet\geq 1}{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{4.13332pt}{$M_{Y}\cap{\mathfrak{m}}^{\bullet}_{X}\cdot M_{X}+{\mathfrak{m}}^{j}_{Y}\cdot M_{Y}$}\!\!\diagup\!\!\raisebox{-4.13332pt}{${\mathfrak{m}}^{j}_{Y}\cdot M_{Y}$}\right.}\quad\text{ for }\quad d\gg 1.

Finally,
\cap_{\bullet}{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$M_{Y}\cap{\mathfrak{m}}^{\bullet}_{X}\cdot M_{X}+{\mathfrak{m}}^{j}_{Y}\cdot M_{Y}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{${\mathfrak{m}}^{j}_{Y}\cdot M_{Y}$}\right.}\subseteq\cap_{\bullet}{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{${\mathfrak{m}}^{\bullet}_{X}\cdot M_{X}+{\mathfrak{m}}^{j}_{Y}\cdot M_{Y}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{${\mathfrak{m}}^{j}_{Y}\cdot M_{Y}$}\right.}\twoheadleftarrow\cap_{\bullet}({\mathfrak{m}}^{\bullet}_{X}\cdot M_{X})=0
.

Thus
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$M_{Y}\cap{\mathfrak{m}}^{d_{j}}\cdot M_{X}+{\mathfrak{m}}_{Y}\cdot M_{Y}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{${\mathfrak{m}}^{j}_{Y}\cdot M_{Y}$}\right.}=0
for
d_{j}\gg 1
. Hence the statement.

\blacksquare

Lemma 6.12
.

Assume
(f)\subseteq I
.

1.

Suppose
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j_{l})}}f\supseteq{\mathfrak{c}}_{l}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
for
l=1,2
and some ideals
{\mathfrak{c}}_{l}\subseteq I^{j_{l}+1}
, with
j_{l}\geq 0
.
Then
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j_{1}+j_{2}+1)}}f\supseteq{\mathfrak{c}}_{1}\cdot{\mathfrak{c}}_{2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.

2.

Suppose
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f\supseteq I^{d_{j}}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
and
T_{{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(i)}}f\supseteq I^{d_{i}}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
for some
d_{j}\geq i\geq 0
and
d_{j}>j\geq-1
.
Then
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j+1)}}f\supseteq I^{d_{j}+d_{i}-i}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.

By iterating this we get:
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j+k)}}f\supseteq I^{d_{j}+k(d_{i}-i)}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
for each
k\geq 0
.

3.

Suppose an ideal
\mathfrak{a}\subset R_{X}
and a subset
\mathfrak{b}_{Y}\subseteq f^{\#}(y)^{2}\cap\mathfrak{a}\subset R_{X}
satisfy:
\mathfrak{a}^{2}\subseteq\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cdot\mathfrak{b}_{Y}
.
Then
\mathfrak{a}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f\supseteq\mathfrak{b}_{Y}\cdot\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\supseteq\mathfrak{a}^{2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.

For a subset
\mathfrak{b}_{Y}\subseteq f^{\#}(y)^{2}\cap(\mathfrak{a}\cdot{\mathfrak{m}})\subset R_{X}
satisfying
\mathfrak{a}^{2}\subseteq\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cdot C
one has:

\mathfrak{a}\cdot{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f\supseteq\mathfrak{b}_{Y}\cdot\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\supseteq\mathfrak{a}^{2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.

4.

Take a subset
C\subseteq(y)\subset R_{Y}
.
Then
C\cdot{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f\supseteq C\cdot\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(o)}}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.

In particular,
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(o)}}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}^{(0)}}f+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f\supseteq(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(o)}}\cap f^{\#}(R_{Y})+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}^{(o)}})\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(o)}}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.

And also:

(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cap f^{\#}(R_{Y})+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}^{(o)}})\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}^{(0)}}f+(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cap y)^{2}\cdot T_{\mathscr{L}}f\supseteq(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cap f^{\#}(R_{Y})+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}^{(o)}})^{2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.

(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cap f^{\#}(R_{Y})+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}})\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}^{(0)}}f+(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cap y)^{2}\cdot T_{\mathscr{L}}f\supseteq(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cap f^{\#}(R_{Y})+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}})^{2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.

5.

Suppose
(f)\subseteq I
then

\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{K}}\!\cdot(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}\cap I^{j+2})\subseteq\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(j+1)}}
for each
j\geq-1
.

If
(f)\subseteq I
and
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f\subseteq T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j+1)}}f
then

\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{K}}\!\cdot\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}\subseteq\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(j+1)}}
for each
j\geq-1
.

We remark that in part 2 the sequence
\{d_{i}-i\}
is non-increasing and
d_{i}-i\geq ord(f)
.

Proof.
All the proofs are direct verifications.

1.

{\mathfrak{c}}_{1}\cdot{\mathfrak{c}}_{2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq{\mathfrak{c}}_{1}\cdot\Big(T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j_{2})}}f+f^{\#}(\mathfrak{b}_{j_{2}})\cdot T_{\mathscr{L}}f\Big)\subseteq T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j_{2}+j_{1}+1)}}f+f^{\#}(\mathfrak{b}_{j_{2}})\cdot\Big(T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j_{1})}}f+

+f^{\#}(\mathfrak{b}_{j_{1}})\cdot T_{\mathscr{L}}f\Big)\subseteq T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j_{1}+j_{2}+1)}}f+f^{\#}(\mathfrak{b}_{j_{1}+j_{2}+1})\cdot T_{\mathscr{L}}f=T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j_{1}+j_{2}+1)}}f.

2.

I^{d_{j}+d_{i}-i}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq I^{d_{j}-i}\cdot\left(T_{\mathscr{R}^{(i)}}f+I^{i}\cdot(f)R^{\oplus p}_{X}\right)\subseteq T_{\mathscr{R}^{(d_{j})}}f+I^{d_{j}}\cdot(f)R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq T_{\mathscr{R}^{(d_{j})}}f+(f)\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}}f+(y)\cdot T_{\mathscr{L}^{(j)}}f\subseteq T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j+1)}}f
.

3.

Multiply the inclusion
T_{\mathscr{A}}f\supseteq\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
by
\mathfrak{b}_{Y}
.

4.

Multiply the inclusion
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(o)}}f\supseteq\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(o)}}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
by
C
.

5.

\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{K}}\!\!\cdot\!(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}\!\cap\!I^{j+2})\!\cdot\!R^{\oplus p}_{X}\!\subseteq\!(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}\!\cap\!I^{j+2})\!\cdot\!(T_{\mathscr{R}}f\!+\!(f)\!\cdot\!R^{\oplus p}_{X})\!\subseteq\!T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j+1)}}f\!+\!(f)\!\cdot\!T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}}f\!+\!(y)\!\cdot\!T_{\mathscr{L}^{(j)}}f\!\subseteq\!T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j+1)}}f.

\blacksquare

Example 6.13
.

i.

Part 1 gives:
\mathfrak{a}^{2}_{\mathscr{A}^{(0)}}\subseteq\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(1)}}
.

ii.

Let
I={\mathfrak{m}}
and
\mathfrak{a}\subseteq T_{\mathscr{A}^{(0)}}f
and
(f)\subseteq\mathfrak{a}
. Then
\mathfrak{a}^{2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq I\cdot{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f
.

6.5.
Filtration closures,
\overline{T_{\mathscr{A}}f}
vs
T_{\mathscr{A}}f
.

The tangent spaces
T_{\mathscr{R}}f,T_{\mathscr{K}}\!f
are filtration-closed, by lemma
3.6
.
As was explained in §
1.6
, the methods of that lemma are not directly applicable to
\mathscr{A}
-equivalence.
We give the
\mathscr{A}
-version. Fix some
j\geq-1
.

Proposition 6.14
.

1.

\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cdot\overline{T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f}=\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cdot T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f
.

2.

Suppose either
R_{X}={\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk[[x]]$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$J$}\right.}
or
f
is of finite singularity type, §
3.6.2
.
Then
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f=\overline{T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f}
.

Proof.

​​1.​​

Observe the obvious inclusions:
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\!\cdot\!T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f\!\subseteq\!\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\!\cdot\!\overline{T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f}\!\subseteq\!\overline{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cdot T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f}.

Note that
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\!\cdot\!T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f\!\subseteq\!R^{\oplus p}_{X}
is an
R_{X}
-module. Therefore it is closed (lemma
3.6
),

\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cdot T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f=\overline{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cdot T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f}
. Hence

\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cdot T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f=\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cdot\overline{T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f}
.

​​2.​​

The case
R_{X}={\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk[[x]]$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$J$}\right.}
is trivial. Suppose
f
is of finite singularity type.

We have the
(f^{\#}(R_{Y})+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}})
-submodule
\overline{T_{\mathscr{A}}f}\subset R^{\oplus p}_{X}
, by lemma
6.4
.
It satisfies:
T_{\mathscr{A}}+I^{d}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\supseteq\overline{T_{\mathscr{A}}f}\supseteq T_{\mathscr{A}}f
for any
d\gg 1
. Therefore
T_{\mathscr{A}}+(I^{d}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X})\cap\overline{T_{\mathscr{A}}f}\supseteq\overline{T_{\mathscr{A}}f}
.

The ring
f^{\#}(R_{Y})+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}
is local Noetherian (lemma
6.4
),
therefore
\overline{T_{\mathscr{A}}f}
is f.g. over this ring (§
2.1
.vi).

As
f
is of finite singularity type,
\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{K}}\!}={\mathfrak{m}}\supseteq I.

For
d\gg 1
one has
I^{d}\subseteq(f)+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}
, by property
6.2
.iv.
By lemma
6.11
one has:

((f^{\#}(y)+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}})^{d}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X})\cap\overline{T_{\mathscr{A}}f}\subseteq(f^{\#}(y)+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}})\cdot\overline{T_{\mathscr{A}}f}
.
Thus we get
T_{\mathscr{A}}f+(f^{\#}(y)+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}})\cdot\overline{T_{\mathscr{A}}f}\supseteq\overline{T_{\mathscr{A}}f}
.
Apply Nakayama to
T_{\mathscr{A}}f
as a f.g. module over
f^{\#}(R_{Y})+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}
.

\blacksquare

Example 6.15
.

Let
R_{X}
be one of
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk[[x]]$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$J$}\right.},{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk\{x\}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$J$}\right.},{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk\langle x\rangle$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$J$}\right.}
, see §
2.1
.ii.
If
f
is
{\mathscr{K}}\!
-finite then
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f=\overline{T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f}
.

7.
Criteria for left-right orbits, “
\mathscr{A}f
vs
T_{\mathscr{A}}f
”

7.1.
An auxiliary result: orbits of unipotent groups are Zariski closed

Let
G
be an algebraic (connected) unipotent group
over a field
\Bbbk.

Suppose
G
acts on the affine space
\mathbb{A}^{N}_{\Bbbk}.

If
\Bbbk
is algebraically closed then the
G
-orbits are Zariski-closed.
This is the Kostant-Rosenlicht theorem, see e.g.
[
F.-S. R.
, Theorem 2.11]
.

For the
\mathscr{A}
-implicit function theorem below (with
\Bbbk
not algebraically closed) we need the following statement, provided by
Mikhail Borovoi.

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
G
be a (algebraic, connected) unipotent group
over a field
\Bbbk
of characteristic 0.
Suppose a line
L\subseteq\mathbb{A}^{N}_{\Bbbk}
intersects an orbit

Gx_{o}\subset\mathbb{A}^{N}_{\Bbbk}
in an infinite number of points.
Then
Gx_{o}\supseteq L
.

Proof.
Take the algebraic closure
\Bbbk\subseteq{\bar{\Bbbk}}
and the corresponding orbit
G_{\bar{\Bbbk}}\cdot x_{o}\subseteq\mathbb{A}^{N}_{\bar{\Bbbk}}.

Thus
G_{\bar{\Bbbk}}\cdot x_{o}
is Zariski-closed (by Kostant-Rosenlicht), hence contains
L
.
The orbit
G_{\bar{\Bbbk}}\cdot x_{o}
contains the
\Bbbk
-point
x_{o}
,
hence is stable under the action of the Galois group
{\rm Gal}(\bar{\Bbbk}/\Bbbk)
.
We conclude (by Galois descent) that
G_{\bar{\Bbbk}}\cdot x_{o}
is defined over
\Bbbk
,
see, e.g.,
[
Jahnel.00
, Proposition 2.6]
.

Denote by
G_{0}
the stabilizer of
x_{o}
in
G
. Then
G_{0}
is a unipotent
\Bbbk
-group.
As
char(\Bbbk)=0
,
G_{0}
is isomorphic, as a variety, to its Lie algebra
{\rm Lie}(G_{0})
and hence is connected,
see, e.g.,
[
B.dP.W.87
, Lemma (3.1)]
.
By
[
Sansuc.81
, Lemma 1.13]
, see also
[
Bo.Da.Re.21
, Proposition 3.1]
,
we have
H^{1}(\Bbbk,G_{0})=1
.
The set of orbits of
G_{\Bbbk}
in
(G_{\bar{\Bbbk}}\cdot x_{o})\cap\mathbb{A}^{N}_{\Bbbk}

is in a canonical bijection with
\ker[H^{1}(\Bbbk,G_{0})\to H^{1}(\Bbbk,G)]
,
see
[
Serre.02
, Section I.5.4, Proposition 36]
.
As
H^{1}(\Bbbk,G_{0})=1
, we see that there is only one orbit in
(G_{\bar{\Bbbk}}\cdot x_{o})\cap\mathbb{A}^{N}_{\Bbbk}
,
that is,
(G_{\bar{\Bbbk}}\cdot x_{o})\cap\mathbb{A}^{N}_{\Bbbk}=G_{\Bbbk}\cdot x_{o}
.
Therefore
G_{\Bbbk}\cdot x_{o}\supseteq L
, as required.

\blacksquare

Lemma
7.1
does not hold in positive characteristic, even if
\Bbbk
is perfect.

Example 7.2
.

(by Zev Rosegarten) Let
\Bbbk
be an infinite field of characteristic
p
, such that the Artin-Schreier map,
\Bbbk\!\ni\!c\!\to\!c^{p}\!-c\!\in\!\Bbbk
is not surjective.
Equivalently,
H^{1}(\Bbbk,\!{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\mathbb{Z}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$p\mathbb{Z}$}\right.})\!\neq\!0.
​ One can even assume that
\Bbbk
is perfect. (E.g. let
\Bbbk
be the perfect closure of a local or global function field.) Take the action (of the additive group)

(45)

G_{a}:=\Bbbk\circlearrowright\mathbb{A}^{2},\quad\lambda\cdot\begin{bmatrix}x_{1}\\
x_{2}\end{bmatrix}:=\begin{bmatrix}1&\lambda^{p}-\lambda\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\cdot\begin{bmatrix}x_{1}\\
x_{2}\end{bmatrix}.

This action is algebraic and unipotent. It preserves the line
x_{2}=1
. And the orbit
G_{a}(0,1)
is infinite.
But this orbit does not contain this line, as the Artin-Schreier map is non-surjective.

7.2.
The ​
\mathscr{A}
-implicit function theorem

Let
R_{X}
be one of
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk[[x]]$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$J$}\right.},{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk\{x\}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$J$}\right.},{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk\langle x\rangle$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$J$}\right.}
,
§
2.1
.ii.
Denote by
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Bbbk}
the maximal ideal of
\Bbbk.

Take a map
f:X\to(\Bbbk^{p},o)
. If its critical locus,
Crit(f)
, see §
3.6
, is a point, i.e.
\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}}={\mathfrak{m}}
, then already theorem
4.1

gives a good control on the orbit, e.g.
\mathscr{R}f\supseteq\{f\}+\mathfrak{a}^{2}_{\mathscr{R}}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
. For
p\geq 2
(and
f
not a submersion)
the critical locus is always of positive dimension. And “in most cases” the instability locus,
V(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}})
, is of
dimension smaller than
Crit(f)
. We adopt the following assumptions.

Assumptions 7.3
.

i.

Take an ideal
{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}\subseteq\mathfrak{a}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}
, so that
V(\mathfrak{a})\subseteq Crit(f).

Suppose either
Crit(f)=V({\mathfrak{m}})\subset X
or the locus
V(\mathfrak{a})\subset Crit(f)
contains no irreducible component of
Crit(f)\subset X
.

ii.

If
J\!\neq\!0
and
\Bbbk\not\supseteq\mathbb{Q}
then we assume the
jet_{0}
-condition of §
2.5
.

Moreover, for the ring
R_{X}\!=\!{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk\{x\}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$J$}\right.}
(with
J\!\neq\!0
) the statements are for the
{\mathfrak{m}}
-adic
closure,
\overline{\mathscr{A}f}\!\supseteq\!\{f\}\!+\!\cdots.

Recall the tangent space of the left equivalence,
T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f=f^{\sharp}((y)^{2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{Y})
, see §
3.3.2
.

Theorem 7.4
.

Suppose
\mathfrak{a}^{2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq\mathfrak{a}\cdot{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f
.

a.​​

Then
