{\mathscr{A}f}\supseteq\{f\}+({\mathfrak{m}}_{\Bbbk}+\mathfrak{a}^{2}+(f))\cdot\mathfrak{a}^{2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f
.

b.​​

Suppose the field
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{${\mathfrak{m}}_{\Bbbk}$}\right.}
is of characteristic zero or is algebraically closed.
​​Then

\mathscr{A}f\!\supseteq\!\{f\}\!+\!\mathfrak{a}^{2}\!\cdot\!R^{\oplus p}_{X}\!+\!T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f.

The trivial choice
\mathfrak{a}={\mathfrak{m}}\cdot\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}
satisfies the assumptions. Then the statement is implied by theorem
4.1
.

We remark: the assumption
\mathfrak{a}^{2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot\mathfrak{a}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f
is preserved by
\mathscr{A}
-equivalence.

Proof.
The proof is an essentially more complicated version of the proofs of theorems
4.1
,
5.1
.

•

The condition
f+g\in\mathscr{A}f
is not an implicit function equation.
Yet, in Step 2 we reduce it to a system of implicit function equations over the ring
R_{Y}
, (
50
),
(
51
), modulo higher order terms,
\mathfrak{a}^{d}_{\mathscr{R}}
for
d\gg 1.
Here the additional tool is the Weierstraß division.

•

Part a., for the case
J=0
, is proved in Step 3 as in the
\mathscr{R},{\mathscr{K}}\!
-cases, by applying
IFT_{1\hskip-2.84544pt\rm I}
.

•

Part b. (Step 4) is more involved. Unlike the
\mathscr{R},{\mathscr{K}}\!
-cases we cannot resolve this system directly.

–

Step 4.i. gives an “arc-solution”,
f+tg\in\mathscr{A}_{t}f
. This arc is formal, with parametrization in
\Bbbk[\![t]\!]
.

–

Step 4.ii. gives: the condition

f\!+\!tg\!\in\!\mathscr{A}_{t}f
is resolvable for an infinite number of values of
t\!\in\!{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{${\mathfrak{m}}_{\Bbbk}$}\right.}.

–

Step 4.iii. To achieve “
f+tg\in\mathscr{A}_{t}f
for any
t\in\Bbbk
” we pass to
finite jets and use the theorem of Kostant-Rosenlicht or Lemma
7.1
.

•

The case
J\neq 0
is then obtained iteratively, using
jet_{o}
-assumption, in a way similar to theorems
4.1
,
5.1
. This gives the formal
\mathscr{A}
-equivalence. In the Nash case,
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk\langle x\rangle$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$J$}\right.},
one invokes the left-right Artin approximation, §
2.3
.v. In the analytic case,
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk\{x\}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$J$}\right.}
, the statement is only for the orbit-closures.

​Step 1.​​

(Preparations)

​​
i.​​

Consider the ​
R_{X}
-module ​
M\!\!:=\!\!{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\mathfrak{a}^{2}\!\cdot\!R^{\oplus p}_{X}\!+\!\mathfrak{a}\!\cdot\!{\mathfrak{m}}\!\cdot\!T_{\mathscr{R}}f$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$\mathfrak{a}\!\cdot\!{\mathfrak{m}}\!\cdot\!T_{\mathscr{R}}f$}\right.}
. ​Take its annihilator
ideal ​
\mathfrak{a}_{M}\!\!:=\!Ann(M)\!\subset\!R_{X}.

We have
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}\!\cdot\!M\!=\!0
, thus
\mathfrak{a}_{M}\supseteq\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}.
Geometrically:
Supp(M)\subseteq Crit(f)=V(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}})\subset X.

We claim:
\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{M}}=\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}}
. This is obvious for
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}={\mathfrak{m}}
(i.e.
Crit(f)=V({\mathfrak{m}})=o\in X
). Thus we assume:
dimCrit(f)>0
and

V(\mathfrak{a})
contains no irreducible component of
Crit(f)
.
Obviously
Supp(M)\setminus V(\mathfrak{a})=V(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}})\setminus V(\mathfrak{a})
. (In fact, over
X\setminus V(\mathfrak{a})
one has:
M\cong T^{1}_{\mathscr{R}}f.
)
And
Supp(M)\subset X
is closed. Therefore
Supp(M)=Crit(f)
, i.e.

\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{M}}=\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}}
.

​​
ii.​​

Consider
M
as an
R_{Y}
-module. By our assumption
M
is a submodule of

{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f+{\mathfrak{m}}\mathfrak{a}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{${\mathfrak{m}}\mathfrak{a}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f$}\right.}
. The later module is finitely generated over
R_{Y}
. Therefore
M
is f.g.
over
R_{Y}
, §
2.1
.vi.

Each
x_{i}\!\in\!R_{X}
acts on
M
as an
R_{Y}
-linear operator. And its action is filtration-nilpotent,
i.e.
x_{i}^{d}\!\cdot\!M\!\subseteq\!f^{\#}(y)\!\cdot\!M
for
d\!\gg\!1
. Therefore the
characteristic polynomial of
x_{i}
is of the form

(46)

\chi_{x_{i}}(t):=det[t{1\hskip-2.84544pt\rm I}-x_{i}]=t^{d_{i}}+y(\dots)\in R_{Y}[t].

One has
\chi_{x_{i}}(x_{i})=0\circlearrowright M
,
by Cayley-Hamilton theorem. (Hence
x^{d_{i}}_{i}\in(f)+\mathfrak{a}_{M}.
)
Therefore we can apply the Weierstraß division, §
2.1
.vii, and present

R_{X}=\mathfrak{a}_{M}+Span_{R_{Y}}\{v_{\bullet}\}
. Here
\{v_{\bullet}\}
is a finite tuple whose image generates the
R_{Y}
-module

{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$R_{X}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$\mathfrak{a}_{M}$}\right.}
.

We take
v_{0}=1
and
v_{l>0}\in{\mathfrak{m}}
.

​​
iii.​​

We claim:
\{f\}+{\mathfrak{c}}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f\subseteq\mathscr{L}(\{f\}+{\mathfrak{c}}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X})
, for any ideal
{\mathfrak{c}}\subset R_{X}
.
Indeed, given an element
q(y)\in T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f
, define the automorphism
\Phi_{Y}\in\mathscr{L}^{(1)}
by
\Phi_{Y}(y)=y+q(y)
.
Then
\Phi^{-1}_{Y}(\{f\}+{\mathfrak{c}}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}+q(f))\subseteq\{f\}+{\mathfrak{c}}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.

In addition
\mathscr{R}f\supseteq\{f\}+{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\cdot\mathfrak{a}^{3}_{\mathscr{R}}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
, by Theorem
4.1
.

In view of the previous observations,
it is enough to prove (for part b.):

(47)

\quad\quad\{f\}+\mathfrak{a}^{2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq\mathscr{A}\Big(\{f\}+\mathfrak{a}^{d}_{M}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f\Big)\quad\quad\quad\text{ for }\quad d\gg 1.

From now and until Step 5, we assume
J=0
, i.e.
R_{X}
is one of the rings
\Bbbk[[x]],\Bbbk\{x\},\Bbbk\langle x\rangle.

​Step 2.​​

(Reduction of the condition (
47
) to a system of implicit function equations)

As in the
\mathscr{R},{\mathscr{K}}\!
-cases we fix some generators
\{a^{M}_{m}\}
of
\mathfrak{a}_{M}
and
\{\xi_{i}(f)\}
of
\mathfrak{a}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f
(as
R_{X}
-modules).
Recall the expansion
R_{X}=\mathfrak{a}_{M}+Span_{R_{Y}}\{v_{\bullet}\}
of Step 1.ii.

​​i.​​

(
The first attempt.
)
Take a perturbation
g\in\mathfrak{a}^{2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
. Expand it,
g-\sum c^{g}_{i}\xi_{i}(f)\in T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f
, here
c^{g}_{i}\in{\mathfrak{m}}
.
Accordingly define the coordinate change
\Phi_{X}\in\mathscr{R}
by
x\to x+\sum c_{i}\xi_{i}(x)
, here
\{c_{i}\}\in R_{X}
are unknowns.
As in the proofs of
\mathscr{R},{\mathscr{K}}\!
-cases we have:

(48)

\Phi_{X}(f)-f-\sum c_{i}\cdot\xi_{i}(f)\in\mathfrak{a}^{2}\!\cdot\!\{c_{*}\}^{2}\!\cdot\!R^{\oplus p}_{X},\quad\quad\quad\Phi_{X}(g)-g\in\big[\mathfrak{a}\!\cdot\!\{c_{*}\}\!\cdot\!(\mathfrak{a}^{2})^{\prime}+\mathfrak{a}^{2}\!\cdot\!(\mathfrak{a}^{2})^{\prime\prime}\{c_{*}\}^{2}\big]\!\cdot\!R^{\oplus p}_{X}.

We cannot use the assumption
\mathfrak{a}^{2}R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot\mathfrak{a}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f
directly, as
{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot\mathfrak{a}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f
is not an
R_{X}
-module. Instead we expand
c_{i}=\sum_{l}c_{i,l}v_{l}+\sum_{m}\tilde{c}_{i,m}\mathfrak{a}^{M}_{m}
, with the unknowns
c_{i,l}\in R_{Y}
,
\tilde{c}_{i,m}\in R_{X}
. (Using
R_{X}=\mathfrak{a}_{M}+Span\{v_{\bullet}\}
.)
Similarly one expands
\{c^{g}_{i}\}
.
The total expression is:

(49)

\Phi_{X}(f+g)=f+f^{\#}(y)^{2}\cdot H(\{c_{**}\})+\hskip 256.0748pt

\hskip 85.35826pt+\sum_{i}\Big[\sum_{l}v_{l}(c_{i,l}+c^{g}_{i,l})+\sum_{m}a^{M}_{m}(\tilde{c}_{i,m}+\tilde{c}^{g}_{i,m})+Q_{i}(\{c_{*}\})+\mathcal{H}_{i}(\{c_{*}\},\{\tilde{c}_{*}\})\Big]\xi_{i}(f).

Here
Q_{i}(z)\in(z)^{2}\cdot R_{Y}[[z]]
,
\mathcal{H}_{i}(z,w)\in(z,w)^{2}\cdot R_{Y}[[z,w]]
and
H\in T_{\mathscr{L}}f
.

We would like to get rid of the term
[\dots]
, i.e. the coefficient of
\xi_{i}(f)
. This cannot be done directly. Instead we will
force this coefficient to belong to
\mathfrak{a}^{d}_{M}
for
d\gg 1
.
This will ensure the condition (
47
).

​​ii.​​

(
The actual reduction.
)
Iterate the expansion
R_{X}=\mathfrak{a}_{M}+Span_{R_{Y}}\{v_{\bullet}\}
to get
R_{X}=\mathfrak{a}^{d}_{M}+\sum^{d-1}_{k=0}\mathfrak{a}^{k}_{M}\cdot Span_{R_{Y}}\{v_{\bullet}\}
,
for any
d\geq 1
. Thus
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$R_{X}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$\mathfrak{a}^{d}_{M}$}\right.}
is a f.g.
R_{Y}
-module. Fix some generators,
R_{X}=\mathfrak{a}^{d}_{M}+Span_{R_{Y}}\{v_{\bullet}\}
.
As before, we take
v_{0}=1
and
v_{l>0}\in{\mathfrak{m}}
. Moreover, we choose the generators in a filtered way:
v_{0},\!\dots,\!v_{j_{1}}
​ generate
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$R_{X}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$\mathfrak{a}_{M}$}\right.}
,​ then

v_{0},\!\dots,\!v_{j_{1}},\!\dots,\!v_{j_{2}}
​ generate
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$R_{X}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$\mathfrak{a}^{2}_{M}$}\right.}
,​ and so on.

As in Step 1.ii., the action
{\mathfrak{m}}\!\circlearrowright\!{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$R_{X}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$\mathfrak{a}^{d}_{M}$}\right.}
is filtration-nilpotent, i.e.
​
{\mathfrak{m}}^{N}\!\subseteq\!(y)\!+\!\mathfrak{a}^{d}_{M}
​ for ​
N\!\gg\!1.

Expand
c_{i}=\sum_{l}v_{l}c_{i,l}
, where
c_{i,l}\in R_{Y}
are the new unknowns.

Repeat the procedure of Step 2.i, then equation (
49
) becomes:

(50)

\Phi_{X}(f+g)-f-\sum_{i,l}v_{l}\cdot\big[c_{i,l}+c^{g}_{i,l}+Q_{i,l}(\{c_{**}\})\big]\cdot\xi_{j}(f)\in\mathfrak{a}^{d}_{M}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f.

Here

Q_{i,l}(z)\in(z)\cdot R_{Y}[[y\cdot z]][z]
, resp.
(z)\cdot R_{Y}\{y\cdot z\}[z]
, resp.
(z)\cdot R_{Y}\langle y\cdot z\rangle[z]
, rather than just

Q_{i,l}(z)\in(z)\cdot R_{Y}[[z]]
.

As
v_{0}=1
and
v_{l>0}\in{\mathfrak{m}}
one has here:
c^{g}_{i,0}\in(y)
and
Q_{i,0}(z)\in(y)\cdot(z)
.

We get the (finite) system of
implicit function equations over the ring
R_{Y}
:

(51)

c_{i,l}+c^{g}_{i,l}+Q_{i,l}(\{c_{**}\})=0,\quad\forall\ i,l.

Once they are satisfied, equation (
50
) will imply equation (
47
).

Unlike the
\mathscr{R},{\mathscr{K}}\!
-cases we cannot immediately apply the
IFT_{1\hskip-2.84544pt\rm I}
of §
2.3
.i.
In fact the coefficients
c^{g}_{i,l}
do not necessarily belong to the maximal ideal
(y)+{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Bbbk}\subset R_{Y}
.
Neither can one assume that
Q_{i,l}(z)
is nilpotent in some sense, e.g.
Q_{i,l}(z)\not\in(f^{\#}(y)+{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Bbbk})\cdot(z)
.

In Step 3 we prove part a., there the system (
51
) is replaced by a simpler system.
To prove part b. (in Step 4) we introduce the indeterminate
t
, and get an “arc-solution”. Then we extend this arc-solution to an ordinary solution.

​Step 3.​​

(The proof of part a. in the case
J\!=\!0
.) The system (
51
) was obtained for the perturbation
g\!\in\!\mathfrak{a}^{2}\!\cdot\!R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.
To prove part a. we start from the perturbation

g\!\in\!({\mathfrak{m}}_{\Bbbk}+(f)+\mathfrak{a}^{2})\!\cdot\!\mathfrak{a}^{2}\!\cdot\!R^{\oplus p}_{X}
. Observe:

(52)

({\mathfrak{m}}_{\Bbbk}+(f)+\mathfrak{a}^{2})\cdot\mathfrak{a}^{2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq({\mathfrak{m}}_{\Bbbk}+(f)+\mathfrak{a}^{2})\cdot\mathfrak{a}\cdot{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f.

Thus we fix some generators
\{\xi_{i}\}
of the
R_{X}
-module
({\mathfrak{m}}_{\Bbbk}+(f)+\mathfrak{a}^{2})\cdot\mathfrak{a}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f
.
Define
\Phi_{X}\in\mathscr{R}
by
x\to x+\sum c_{i}\xi_{i}(x)
. We get:

(53)

\Phi_{X}(f+g)-f-\sum c_{i}\xi_{i}(f)\in({\mathfrak{m}}_{\Bbbk}+(f)+\mathfrak{a}^{2})^{2}\cdot\mathfrak{a}^{2}\cdot\{c_{*}\}^{2}+\hskip 142.26378pt

\hskip 85.35826pt+({\mathfrak{m}}_{\Bbbk}+(f)+\mathfrak{a}^{2})\cdot\mathfrak{a}^{2}\cdot(({\mathfrak{m}}_{\Bbbk}+(f)+\mathfrak{a}^{2})\mathfrak{a}^{2}\cdot)^{\prime}\{c_{*}\}\subseteq({\mathfrak{m}}_{\Bbbk}+(f)+\mathfrak{a}^{4})\cdot\mathfrak{a}\cdot{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f.

Expand
c_{i}=\sum c_{i,l}v_{l}
and note that the ideal
({\mathfrak{m}}_{\Bbbk}+(f)+\mathfrak{a}^{4})
acts nilpotently on the filtration of
\{v_{*}\}
.

We get again equation (
51
). However, now
Q_{i,l}(z)\in({\mathfrak{m}}_{k}+(y))+Nilp
, where
the (non-linear) operator
Nilp
is filtration-nilpotent on
\{v_{*}\}
.
And this system is indeed resolvable by the
IFT_{1\hskip-2.84544pt\rm I}
.

Once it is resolved, we have the transition
\{f\}+\mathfrak{a}^{2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\ \lower 1.72218pt\hbox{$\mathrel{\mathop{\rightsquigarrow}\limits^{\mathscr{R}}}$}\ \{f\}+\mathfrak{a}^{d}_{M}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f.

By Step 1.iii. this implies the statement a.

​Step 4.​​

(The proof of part b. in the case
J=0.
)

i.

Extend the base ring
\Bbbk
to
\Bbbk[[t]]
, resp.
\Bbbk\{t\}
, resp.
\Bbbk\langle t\rangle
, for an indeterminate
t
. Instead of
f+g
take
f+tg
.
Accordingly define the coordinate change
\Phi_{X}
by
x\to x+t\sum c_{il}v_{l}\xi_{i}(x)
.
Repeat Step 2.ii up to equation (
50
):

(54)

\Phi_{X}(f+g)-f-t\sum_{i,l}v_{l}\cdot\big[c_{i,l}+c^{g}_{i,l}+t\cdot Q_{i,l}(\{c_{**}\})\big]\xi_{i}(f)\ \in\ \mathfrak{a}^{d}_{M}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}+t\cdot\{c_{**}\}^{2}\cdot T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f.

Then the system (
51
) becomes

c_{i,l}+c^{g}_{i,l}+t\cdot Q_{i,l}(\{c_{**}\})=0.

Applying
IFT_{1\hskip-2.84544pt\rm I}
over the ring
\Bbbk[[y,t]]
, resp.
\Bbbk\{y,t\}
, resp.
\Bbbk\langle y,t\rangle
, we get the solution
\{c_{i,l}(y,t)\}
.
As
c^{g}_{i,0}\in(y)
and
Q_{i,0}(z)\in(y)\cdot(z)
, we get:
c_{i,0}(y,t)\in(y).

For this solution we define the coordinate change
\Phi_{X}\in\mathscr{R}_{t}
by
x\to x+t\sum c_{i,l}\cdot v_{l}\cdot\xi_{i}(x)
.
Observe: this coordinate change is unipotent.
Indeed,
\xi(x)\in\mathfrak{a}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}(x)=\mathfrak{a}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}
and
c_{i,0}\in(y)
and
v_{l>0}\in{\mathfrak{m}}
.

Altogether, we have proved:
\Phi_{X}(f+tg)\in\{f\}+\mathfrak{a}^{d}_{M}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f
, for an indeterminate
t
and any
d\gg 1
.
Now part iii. of Step 1 ensures:

f+t\cdot g\ \lower 1.72218pt\hbox{$\mathrel{\mathop{\sim}\limits^{\mathscr{A}^{(1)}_{t}}}$}\ f
.

An aside remark, taking
t={\mathfrak{m}}_{\Bbbk}+(y)
gives
\{f\}+\mathfrak{a}^{2}\cdot({\mathfrak{m}}_{\Bbbk}+(y))\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq\mathscr{A}f
, which is a weaker version of part a.

From now on we assume: the field
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{${\mathfrak{m}}_{\Bbbk}$}\right.}
is of characteristic zero or algebraically closed. Moreover, ​we ​assume
\{Q_{**}(z)\}\!\not\subset({\mathfrak{m}}_{k}\!+\!(y))\cdot(z).

(Otherwise ​the ​system (
51
) is ​directly resolvable.)

ii.

We prove:
f+tg\ \lower 1.72218pt\hbox{$\mathrel{\mathop{\sim}\limits^{\mathscr{A}}}$}\ f
for an infinite set of values of
t\in\Bbbk
. We still have to resolve the
system
c_{i,l}+c^{g}_{i,l}+t\cdot Q_{i,l}(\{c_{**}\})=0
, now for a fixed
t\in\Bbbk
. Here
Q_{i,l}(z)
are power series in
z
,
not polynomials. However, it is enough to resolve this system over the quotient ring
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$R_{Y}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$(y)+{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Bbbk}$}\right.}
. Indeed,
Q_{i,l}\in R_{Y}[[y\cdot z]][z]

(resp. analytic, resp. algebraic). And
if
\{c^{0}_{**}\}
is a solution
mod(y+{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Bbbk})
,
then by shifting the variables,
c_{**}\rightsquigarrow c_{**}-c^{0}_{**}
, we get the system of
IFT_{1\hskip-2.84544pt\rm I}
-type,

c_{i,l}=\tilde{Q}_{i,l}(c_{**})
, where now
\tilde{Q}_{i,l}(z)\in((y)+{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Bbbk})\cdot(z)
.
(This later system is readily resolvable.)

Over the ring
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$R_{Y}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$(y)+{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Bbbk}$}\right.}
we get a polynomial system in variables
\{c_{i,l}\}
, over the (infinite) field
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{${\mathfrak{m}}_{\Bbbk}$}\right.}
.
This system defines a closed algebraic subscheme of a finite dimensional affine space
\mathbb{A}^{N}_{{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{${\mathfrak{m}}_{\Bbbk}$}\right.}}
.
In Step 4.i we have constructed an arc on this subscheme,
\{c_{i,l}(t)\}
.
This arc is non-trivial (i.e. not a point) as
\{Q_{**}(z)\}\not\subset{\mathfrak{m}}_{k}+(y)
.
Thus the set of
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{${\mathfrak{m}}_{\Bbbk}$}\right.}
-points of this (closed, affine, algebraic) subscheme is of positive dimension.

Moreover, the projection of this scheme onto the line
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{t}
has image of positive dimension.
Hence, as the field
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{${\mathfrak{m}}_{\Bbbk}$}\right.}
is infinite, the system is solvable for an infinite number of
t
-values.
Altogether,
f+tg\ \lower 1.72218pt\hbox{$\mathrel{\mathop{\sim}\limits^{\mathscr{A}}}$}\ f
for an infinite set of values of
t\in\Bbbk
.

iii.

Finally we deduce:
f+tg\ \lower 1.72218pt\hbox{$\mathrel{\mathop{\sim}\limits^{\mathscr{A}}}$}\ f
for any
t\in\Bbbk
. As in Step 4.ii, it is enough to resolve the system over the ring

{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$R_{Y}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$(y)+{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Bbbk}$}\right.}
.

Through the whole proof we have used only the unipotent subgroup
\mathscr{A}^{(1)}<\mathscr{A}
, for the filtration
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\bullet}\!\subset\!R_{X}.

​​Passing to the quotient
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$R_{X}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{${\mathfrak{m}}_{\Bbbk}\!+\!{\mathfrak{m}}^{d}$}\right.}
for
d\!\!\gg\!\!1,

we get the action on the finite dimensional vector space,
\mathscr{A}^{(1)}\!\circlearrowright\!({\mathfrak{m}}\!\cdot\!{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$R_{X}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{${\mathfrak{m}}_{\Bbbk}\!+\!{\mathfrak{m}}^{d}$}\right.})^{\oplus p}
. This action is
algebraic and unipotent.

If the field
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{${\mathfrak{m}}_{\Bbbk}$}\right.}
is algebraically closed then the group-orbits are Zariski-closed, §
7.1
.
The intersection of such an orbit with a line,
\mathscr{A}^{(1)}(f)\cap\{f+tg\}_{t}
, is either finite, or contains the whole line.
In our case this intersection is infinite, thus
\mathscr{A}^{(1)}(f)\supseteq f+tg
for each
t\in\Bbbk
.

The case “
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{${\mathfrak{m}}_{\Bbbk}$}\right.}
is of characteristic zero” is done similarly, using lemma

7.1
.

​Step 5.​​

(The case
J\neq 0.
We prove part b., as part a. is simpler.)
In Steps 3,4 we have constructed the map
\Phi_{X}:x\to x+\sum c_{i}\xi_{i}(x)
satisfying
\Phi_{Y}\circ f\circ\Phi_{X}=f+g
.
For
J\neq 0
this map is not a ring-automorphism. As in the
\mathscr{R},{\mathscr{K}}\!
-cases we adjust it by higher order terms.
To use the
jet_{0}
-assumption we expand further,
c_{i,l}=\sum{\underline{y}}^{\underline{m}}c_{il{\underline{m}}}
,
now
c_{il{\underline{m}}}\in\Bbbk
are unknowns.
Then we extend
\Phi_{X}
to the automorphism
\Psi:x\to x+t\sum{\underline{y}}^{\underline{m}}c_{il{\underline{m}}}v_{l}\xi_{i}(x)+t^{2}\varphi(\{c_{***}\}^{2})
.
Here the entries of
\varphi(\{c_{***}\}^{2})
are power series in
\{c_{***}\}
. Resolve the corresponding system with
t
-indeterminate.
This is an arc solution on the variety in
\mathbb{A}^{N}_{\Bbbk}
.
Arguing as in Step 4.ii we get:
f+tg\in\mathscr{A}^{(1)}_{t}(\{f\}+\mathfrak{a}^{d}_{M}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}).

Proceed as in Step 4.iii to get:
f+g\in\mathscr{A}^{(1)}(\{f\}+\mathfrak{a}^{d}_{M}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}).
This holds for any
d\geq 1
.
Therefore
f+g\in\overline{\mathscr{A}^{(1)}f}.

This proves the statement for the ring
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk[[x]]$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$J$}\right.}.
For the ring
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk\langle x\rangle$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$J$}\right.}
one uses the
left-right Artin approximation, §
2.3
.v.

\blacksquare

Remark 7.5
.

The factor
{\mathfrak{m}}
in the assumption
\mathfrak{a}^{2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq\mathfrak{a}\cdot{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f+y^{2}\cdot T_{\mathscr{L}}f
is used
only once in this whole proof, in Step 4.i.
It is needed to ensure that the coordinate change
\Phi_{X}:x\to x+t\sum c_{i,l}v_{l}\xi_{i}(x)
is unipotent.
But this unipotence
holds trivially if one assumes
\mathfrak{a}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}
. Thus (almost) the same proof leads to the modified part b.:

If
\mathfrak{a}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}
and
\mathfrak{a}^{2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq\mathfrak{a}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f
then

\{f\}+\mathfrak{a}^{2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f\subseteq\mathscr{A}f
.

7.3.
Examples and corollaries

Let
R_{X}
be one of
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk[[x]]$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$J$}\right.},{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk\{x\}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$J$}\right.},{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk\langle x\rangle$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$J$}\right.}
, where
\Bbbk
is a local ring (e.g. any field), §
2.1
.ii.
Suppose the field
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{${\mathfrak{m}}_{\Bbbk}$}\right.}
is algebraically closed or of characteristic zero.
Take the assumptions
7.3
.

We use the filtration
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\bullet}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
and the corresponding tangent spaces

T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}},T_{\mathscr{L}^{(j)}},T_{{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(j)}},T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}},
see §
3.3
.

7.3.1.
The case
p=1
, i.e. the scalar valued functions

Let
f:X\to(\Bbbk^{1},o)
and suppose
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}.

Then

\mathscr{A}f\supseteq\{f\}+\mathfrak{a}^{2}_{\mathscr{A}}+Span_{\Bbbk}(f^{2},f^{3},f^{4},\dots).

Proof.
Combine lema
6.1
with remark
7.5
.
\blacksquare

7.3.2.
Maps whose
\mathscr{A}
and
{\mathscr{K}}\!
orbits are “close”

​i.​

Suppose
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(o)}}\supseteq{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot(f)
, equivalently
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(o)}}f=T_{{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(o)}}f
, equivalently
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(o)}}=\mathfrak{a}_{{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(o)}}
.
(This holds, e.g. for infinitesimally-stable maps, see Property
6.2
.ii.)
Then

(55)

\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(o)}}^{2}R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(o)}}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}^{(0)}}f+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(o)}}\cdot T_{\mathscr{L}^{(0)}}f\subseteq\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(o)}}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}^{(0)}}f+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f.

Part b. of Theorem
7.4
gives (for
\mathfrak{a}=\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(0)}}
):
\{f\}+\mathfrak{a}_{{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(o)}}^{2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq\mathscr{A}f
.
This is close to Mather’s statement “Stable maps are determined by their local algebras”.

Note: it is much simpler to compute/to control the ideal
\mathfrak{a}_{{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(o)}}
than
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(o)}}
.

​ii.​

As a particular case, suppose
J=0
and take a stable map.
It is a versal unfolding of its genotype, i.e.
f:(\Bbbk^{n+\tau-1},o)\to(\Bbbk^{p+\tau-1},o)
with
f=(f_{o}+\sum v_{j}u_{j},u)
,
where
f_{o}:(\Bbbk^{n},o)\to(\Bbbk^{p},o)
and
Span_{\Bbbk}(\{v_{\bullet}\})={\mathfrak{m}}\cdot T^{1}_{\mathscr{K}}\!f_{o}
.
(Thus
\tau=dimT^{1}_{\mathscr{K}}\!f_{o}
.)
For
\Bbbk=\mathbb{R},\mathbb{C}
this is the classical Mather’s theorem,
[
Mon. Nuñ.-Bal.
, Theorem 7.2]
, for an arbitrary field see
[
Kerner.22
]
.

We have
\mathfrak{a}^{f}_{{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(o)}}\!=\!\mathfrak{a}^{f_{o}}_{{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(o)}}\!+\!(u)
, see example
3.14
.vi.
Thus we get
\mathscr{A}f\!\supset\!\{f\}\!+\!(\mathfrak{a}^{f_{o}}_{{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(o)}}+(u))^{2}\!\cdot\!R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.
This is much more useful than the classical bound
\mathscr{A}f\supset\{f\}+{\mathfrak{m}}^{p+\tau}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.
As we have seen in examples
4.4
,
5.2
, in most cases the ideal
(\mathfrak{a}^{f_{o}}_{{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(o)}}+(u))^{2}
is larger than
{\mathfrak{m}}^{p+\tau}
.
(Even the asymptotics is better.)

​iii.​

More generally, for
J=0
, suppose
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}\supseteq{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot(f)^{j+1}
for some
j\geq 0
. (Thus
\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}}=\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(j)}}}.
)
Then

(56)

\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}^{2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}+(f)^{j+1})\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}}f+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}\cdot T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f\subseteq\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}}f+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f.

Therefore
\{f\}+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}^{2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq\mathscr{A}f
.

Yet more generally, it is enough to assume
(f)^{j+1}\!\cdot\!\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}\!\cdot\!R^{\oplus p}_{X}\!\subseteq\!\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}}f\!+\!T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f
for some
j\!\geq\!0.

7.3.3.

​​​​The main assumption of theorem
7.4
is:
\mathfrak{a}^{2}\!\cdot\!R^{\oplus p}_{X}\!\subseteq\!\mathfrak{a}\!\cdot\!{\mathfrak{m}}\!\cdot\!T_{\mathscr{R}}f+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f.
This can be difficult to verify.

Corollary 7.6
.

(A weaker but simpler version)

​1.​​

Suppose
\mathfrak{b}^{2}_{Y}\!\subseteq\!\mathfrak{b}_{Y}\!\cdot\!\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(o)}}
for an ideal
\mathfrak{b}_{Y}\!\subseteq\!f^{\#}(y).

Then
\{f\}\!+\!(\mathfrak{b}_{Y}\!+\!\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}^{(o)}})^{4}\!\cdot\!R^{\oplus p}_{X}\!+\!T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f\!\subseteq\!\mathscr{A}f.

Moreover, if the field
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{${\mathfrak{m}}_{\Bbbk}$}\right.}
is algebraically closed or of characteristic zero then

\{f\}+(\mathfrak{b}_{Y}+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}^{(o)}})^{2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f\subseteq\mathscr{A}f.

​2.​​

Suppose
\mathfrak{b}^{2}_{Y}\subseteq\mathfrak{b}_{Y}\cdot\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}
for an ideal
\mathfrak{b}_{Y}\subseteq f^{\#}(y)^{2}
. Suppose
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}
.

Then
\{f\}+(\mathfrak{b}_{Y}+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}})^{4}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f\subseteq\mathscr{A}f.

Moreover, if the field
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{${\mathfrak{m}}_{\Bbbk}$}\right.}
is algebraically closed or of characteristic zero then

\{f\}+(\mathfrak{b}_{Y}+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}})^{2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f\subseteq\mathscr{A}f.

Proof.

​1.​​

It is enough to observe:
(\mathfrak{b}_{Y}+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}^{(o)}})^{2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq(\mathfrak{b}_{Y}+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}^{(o)}})\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}^{(0)}}+\mathfrak{b}_{Y}^{2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq(\mathfrak{b}_{Y}+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}^{(o)}})\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}^{(0)}}+\mathfrak{b}_{Y}\cdot T_{\mathscr{L}^{(0)}}
. Then we apply theorem
7.4
.

​2.​​

The same proof, just use remark
7.5
.

\blacksquare

Example 7.7
.

(Assuming the field
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{${\mathfrak{m}}_{\Bbbk}$}\right.}
is algebraically closed or of characteristic zero)

​i.​

Take
\mathfrak{b}_{Y}=\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(o)}}\cap f^{\#}(y)
to get
\mathscr{A}f\supset\{f\}+(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(o)}}\cap f^{\#}(y)+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}^{(0)}})^{2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f
.

Suppose
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}
.
Then part two gives:
\mathscr{A}f\supset\{f\}+(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cap f^{\#}(y)^{2}+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}})^{2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f
.

More generally, if
\mathfrak{b}^{2}_{Y}\subseteq\mathfrak{b}_{Y}\cdot\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(o)}}
then the ideal
\mathfrak{b}_{Y}+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}^{(o)}}\cap f^{\#}(y)
satisfies this condition as well.

​ii.​

Suppose
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\supseteq{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot(f)^{d}
, then we have
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cap f^{\#}(y)\supseteq f^{\#}(y)^{d}
.
Assuming
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}\subset{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}
we get

\{f\}+((f)^{d}+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}})^{2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f\subseteq\mathscr{A}f
.

We remark:
\sqrt{(f)^{d}+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}^{(o)}}}=\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{K}}\!}
, thus in this case the (
\mathscr{A}
) instability and the (
{\mathscr{K}}\!
) singularity loci coincide.

See §
7.5
for the geometric interpretation.

7.3.4.
Computational cases

i.

Define the map
f:(\Bbbk^{2},o)\to(\Bbbk^{3},o)
by
f(x_{1},x_{2})=(x_{1},x_{2}^{2},x_{2}^{3}+x_{1}^{k+1}x_{2})
.
Suppose
2,3,k+1\in\Bbbk^{\times}
.
By the direct check:
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}=(x_{1}^{k},x_{2}^{2})\subset R_{X}
and
\mathfrak{a}^{2}_{\mathscr{A}}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f+T_{\mathscr{L}}f
.
Thus
\mathscr{A}f\supseteq\{f\}+(x_{1}^{k},x_{2}^{2})^{2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f
.
Compare this to the classical criterion:
{\mathfrak{m}}^{k+1}\subseteq\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}
and therefore
\mathscr{A}f\supset\{f\}+{\mathfrak{m}}^{2k+2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
,

[
Mon. Nuñ.-Bal.
, Proposition 6.1, pg. 197]
.

ii.

Define the map
f:(\Bbbk^{2},o)\to(\Bbbk^{3},o)
by
f(x_{1},x_{2})=(x_{1},x_{2}^{2},x_{1}x_{2}^{k})
, with
k
-odd.
Suppose
2,k\in\Bbbk^{\times}
. Note that
f^{\#}(y)\supset(x_{1}x_{2}^{k-1})\subset R_{X}
.
By the direct check:
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}=(x_{2}^{k},x_{1}x_{2}^{k-1})\subset R_{X}
. This is a non-isolated instability.

By the direct check:
\mathfrak{a}^{2}_{\mathscr{A}}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f+T_{\mathscr{L}}f
.
Thus
\mathscr{A}f\supseteq\{f\}+(x_{2}^{k},x_{1}x_{2}^{k-1})^{2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(1)}}f
.

7.4.
The filtration criterion for
\mathscr{A}
-orbits

Take an ideal
I\subset R_{X}
and the filtration
M_{\bullet}:=I^{\bullet}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}.

Theorem 7.8
.

Fix some integers
1\leq j<d
.
Assume one of the following:

i.

either the conditions
jet_{N}(Exp)
,
jet_{N}(Ln)
of §
2.5
hold for all
N\geq 1
. (Thus in particular
\Bbbk\supseteq\mathbb{Q}
.)

ii.

or
(f)\subseteq I
and the conditions
jet_{N}(Exp)
,
jet_{N}(Ln)
hold for
N=\lceil\frac{2d-1-ord(f)}{j}\rceil+1
.

Then:
\overline{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}f}\supseteq\{f\}+I^{d}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
if and only if
\overline{T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f}\supseteq I^{d}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.

Proof.
The part
\Lleftarrow
.

The case
i
.

Let
w_{d}\in M_{d}
then
w_{d}\equiv\xi_{Y}(y)|_{f}+\xi_{X}(f)\ mod\ M_{d^{\prime}}
for some
(\xi_{Y},\xi_{X})\in T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f
.
We can assume
d^{\prime}\gg d
.
Using the
jet_{N}(Exp)
-assumption, for
N=\lceil\frac{d-ord(f)}{j}\rceil+1
,
we take the corresponding automorphisms
jet_{N}(e^{\xi_{X}})\in\mathscr{R}^{(j)}
,
jet_{N}(e^{\xi_{Y}})\in\mathscr{L}^{(j)}
.

By remark
3.11
, applied to
M_{d}
and
\mathscr{A}^{(j)}
,
we have:

jet_{N}(e^{\xi_{X}})\cdot jet_{N}(e^{\xi_{Y}})f-f-w_{d}\in M_{d+j}
.
Therefore
\mathscr{A}^{(j)}f+M_{d+j}\supseteq\{f\}+M_{d}
. Now apply the same argument to the pair
(M_{d+j},\mathscr{A}^{(j)})
,
with
jet_{N}(Exp)
-assumption for
N=\lceil\frac{d+j-ord(f)}{j}\rceil+1
. Iterate this to get

\mathscr{A}^{(j)}f+{M_{d^{\prime}}}\supseteq\{f\}+M_{d}
for every
d^{\prime}\gg 1
. Hence
\{f\}+M_{d}\subseteq\overline{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}f}
.

The case
ii
.

It is enough to prove:
\mathscr{A}^{(j)}f+M_{k+1}\supset\{f\}+M_{k}
for each
k\geq d
.
For this we prove:

(57)

\mathscr{A}^{(kj+k-1)}f+M_{kd+l+1}\supseteq\{f\}+M_{kd+l},\quad\text{ for each }\ k\geq 1\ \text{ and each }\ \ l\in 0,\dots,d-1.

At each step we work modulo
M_{d^{\prime}}
with
d^{\prime}\gg 1
, thus we can replace
\overline{T_{\mathscr{A}^{(\bullet)}}}f
by
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(\bullet)}}f
.

By part one of lemma
6.12
we get
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(kj+k-1)}}f\supseteq M_{kd}
for each
k\geq 1
.

Take any element
w\in M_{kd+l}
and present it
as
w=\xi_{X}(f)+\xi_{Y}(y)|_{f}\in T_{\mathscr{A}^{(kj+k-1)}}f
.
Take the corresponding automorphisms
jet_{N}(e^{\xi_{X}})
,
jet_{N}(e^{\xi_{Y}})
, ensured by the condition
jet_{N}(Exp)
.

Apply remark
3.11
to
M_{kd+l}
and
\mathscr{A}^{(kj+k-1)}
to get:

jet_{N}(e^{\xi_{X}})\cdot jet_{N}(e^{\xi_{Y}})f-f-w\in M_{(kd+l)+(kj+k-1)}.

This verifies the condition (
57
).

We should only justify the use of remark
3.11
, i.e. to verify the condition:

(58)

\frac{kd+l-ord(f)}{kj+k-1}+1\leq\lceil\frac{2d-1-ord(f)}{j}\rceil+1\quad\text{ for all $l=0,\dots,d-1$, and all $k,j\geq 1$.}

Here the maximum
of the left hand side is achieved for
l=d-1
and
k=1
. And then we get the equality.

The part
\Rrightarrow
.

The case

i
.

Take
w_{d}\in M_{d}
then
f+w_{d}\equiv(\Phi_{Y},\Phi_{X})f\ mod\ M_{d^{\prime}}
for some
(\Phi_{Y},\Phi_{X})\in\mathscr{A}^{(j)}
. We can assume
d^{\prime}\gg d
.
Using the
jet_{N}
-assumptions we approximate
(\Phi_{Y},\Phi_{X})
by
the elements
jet_{N}(e^{\xi_{X}})\in Aut^{(j)}_{X}
,
jet_{N}(e^{\xi_{Y}})\in Aut^{(j)}_{Y}
satisfying:

(59)

\Phi_{X}-jet_{N}(e^{\xi_{X}})\in End^{(jN+1)}_{\Bbbk}(R_{X}),\quad\quad\quad\Phi_{Y}-jet_{N}(e^{\xi_{Y}})\in End^{(jN+1)}_{\Bbbk}(R_{Y}).

Therefore
(jet_{N}(e^{\xi_{X}}),jet_{N}(e^{\xi_{Y}}))f-f-w_{d}\in M_{d^{\prime}}+M_{jN+1+ord(f)}
. Here we can assume
d^{\prime}\geq jN+1+ord(f)\geq d+j
.
Apply remark
3.11
to the pair
(M_{d},\mathscr{A}^{(j)})

to get:
(\xi_{Y}+\xi_{X})f-w_{d}\in M_{d+j}
.
Therefore
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f+M_{d+j}\supseteq M_{d}
.

Now apply the same argument to the pair
(M_{d+j},\mathscr{A}^{(j)})
, and so on.
One gets
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f+M_{d^{\prime}}\supseteq M_{d}
for any
d^{\prime}
. Hence the statement.

The case

ii
.

Apply the argument of case i. to the pair
(M_{d},\mathscr{A}^{(j)})
and
N=\lceil\frac{d-ord(f)}{j}\rceil+1
to get:
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f+M_{d+j}\supseteq M_{d}
.
Iterate this argument for all pairs
(M_{d+l},\mathscr{A}^{(j)})
and
N=\lceil\frac{d+l-ord(f)}{j}\rceil+1
,
with
l=1,\dots,d-1
. We reach
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f+M_{2d}\supseteq M_{d}
, and for this we have
used the
jet_{N}
-assumptions with
N=\lceil\frac{d-ord(f)}{j}\rceil+1
, …,

\lceil\frac{2d-1-ord(f)}{j}\rceil+1
.

And now observe:

M_{2d}=I^{d}\cdot M_{d}\subseteq I^{d}(T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f+M_{2d})\subseteq I^{d}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}^{(j)}}f+f^{\#}(\mathfrak{b}_{j})T_{\mathscr{L}^{(j)}}f+M_{3d}\ \lower 1.72218pt\hbox{$\mathrel{\mathop{\subseteq}\limits^{(f)\subseteq I}}$}\ T_{\mathscr{A}^{(2j)}}f+M_{3d}.

Iterate this to get
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f+M_{d^{\prime}}\supseteq M_{d}
for all
d^{\prime}
. Hence the statement.

\blacksquare

7.4.1.

The conclusion of theorem
7.8
is for filtration closures, “
\overline{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}f}
vs
\overline{T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f}
”.
With additional assumptions we get the statement “
{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}f}
vs
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f
”.

Let
R_{X}
be one of
{\Bbbk[[x]]},{\Bbbk\{x\}},{\Bbbk\langle x\rangle}
, where the local ring
\Bbbk
contains a field, §
2.1
.ii.
Fix some integers
1\!\leq\!j\!<\!d
.
Suppose either
char(\Bbbk)=0
or

char(\Bbbk)\!\!>\!\!\lceil\frac{2d-1-ord(f)}{j}\rceil\!+\!1
.
Take an ideal
(f)\subseteq I\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}
.

Corollary 7.9
.

​1.​​

Suppose
f
is
{\mathscr{K}}\!
-finite. If

\mathscr{A}^{(j)}f\supseteq\{f\}+I^{d}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
then
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f\supseteq I^{d}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.

​2.​​

Suppose
I\subseteq\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}+(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cap f^{\#}(y))R_{X}}.

If
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}f\supseteq I^{d}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
then

\mathscr{A}^{(j)}f\supseteq\{f\}+I^{d}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}.

Geometrically the condition
I\subseteq\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}+(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cap f^{\#}(y))R_{X}}
reads:
V(I)\supseteq f^{-1}(f(V(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}})))\cap Crit(f)
.

For
\Bbbk\{x\}
with
\Bbbk=\mathbb{R},\mathbb{C}
,
I={\mathfrak{m}}
,
j=1,
this is Theorem 2.5 of
[
B.dP.W.87
]
.

Proof.
W.l.o.g. we assume
I\neq 0
, thus
V(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}})\subsetneq(\Bbbk^{n},o)
.

​1.​​

By theorem
7.8
we have
\overline{T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}}f\supseteq I^{d}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.
Then
{T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}}}f\supseteq I^{d}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
, by part two of lemma
6.14
.

​2.​​

By theorem
7.8
it is enough to show:
\mathscr{A}^{(j)}f\supseteq\{f\}+I^{N}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
for some
N\gg 1
.
Part 2 of corollary
7.6
gives (for
\mathfrak{b}_{Y}=\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cap f^{\sharp}(y)^{2}
):

\{f\}+I^{N}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq\{f\}+(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cap f^{\sharp}(y)^{2})^{4}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subset\mathscr{A}f
.

Moreover, (for
N\gg 1
) in the proof of theorem
7.4
we get the coefficients
c_{**}\in I^{j}.

Therefore the needed coordinate change is an
\mathscr{R}^{(j)}
-element. Similarly,
the involved
\mathscr{L}
-elements are in fact
\mathscr{L}^{(j)}
elements. Therefore

\mathscr{A}^{(j)}f\supseteq\{f\}+I^{N}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.

\blacksquare

Example 7.10
.

Let
R_{X}
be one of
\Bbbk[[x]]
,
\Bbbk\{x\}
,
\Bbbk\langle x\rangle.

Take the filtration
I^{\bullet}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
and the corresponding pairs
(T_{\mathscr{A}^{(j)}},\mathscr{A}^{(j)})
of §
3.3
.
Assume
(f)\subseteq I
and
\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{K}}\!}\supseteq I.

i.

Suppose
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(0)}}f\supseteq I^{d_{\mathscr{A}}}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
and
T_{{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(0)}}f\supseteq I^{d_{\mathscr{K}}\!}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}.

Assume either
char(\Bbbk)=0
or
char(\Bbbk)>2(d_{\mathscr{A}}+d_{\mathscr{K}}\!)-ord(f).
Then
\mathscr{A}^{(1)}f\supseteq\{f\}+I^{d_{\mathscr{A}}+d_{\mathscr{K}}\!}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}.

Proof.
By part 2 of lemma
6.12
we get:
T_{\mathscr{A}^{(1)}}f\supseteq I^{d_{\mathscr{A}}+d_{\mathscr{K}}\!}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
.
Thus
\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}}\supseteq I\supseteq(f).
Hence
\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}+(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cap f^{\sharp}(y))R_{X}}=\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}+(f)}=\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{K}}\!}\supseteq I.

(See example
3.14
.iii.)

Finally, apply Part 2 of corollary
7.9
.

\blacksquare

ii.

We compare the conclusion
\mathscr{A}^{(1)}f\supseteq\{f\}+I^{d_{\mathscr{A}}+d_{\mathscr{K}}\!}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
to the classical results (for
\Bbbk=\mathbb{R},\mathbb{C}
):

•

[
Gaffney-du Plessis.82
, Theorems 0.2 and 2.1]
: Assume
f
is
{\mathscr{K}}\!
-finite.
If
{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(0)}}f\supseteq I^{d}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
then

\mathscr{A}f\supseteq\{f\}+I^{2d+2}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}.

•

[
Mon. Nuñ.-Bal.
, ​Theorem 6.2]
: If
{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot T_{\mathscr{R}}f+T_{\mathscr{L}^{(0)}}f\!\supseteq\!{\mathfrak{m}}^{d}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
then

\mathscr{A}f\!\supseteq\!\{f\}+{\mathfrak{m}}^{2d}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}.

•

[
Mon. Nuñ.-Bal.
, ​​Corollary ​6.3]
: ​If
T_{\mathscr{A}}f\!\!\supseteq\!{\mathfrak{m}}^{d_{\mathscr{A}}}\!\cdot\!R^{\oplus p}_{X}
and
T_{{\mathscr{K}}\!}f\!\!\supseteq\!{\mathfrak{m}}^{d_{\mathscr{K}}\!}\!\cdot\!R^{\oplus p}_{X}
then
​
\mathscr{A}\!f\!\!\supseteq\!\{f\}\!+\!{\mathfrak{m}}^{d_{\mathscr{A}}+d_{\mathscr{K}}\!+1}\!\cdot\!R^{\oplus p}_{X}.

For most types of maps (if one is far from a stable map) one has: if
I^{d}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq T_{\mathscr{A}}f
then

{\mathfrak{m}}^{d}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}\subseteq T_{\mathscr{A}^{(0)}}f\subsetneq T_{{\mathscr{K}}\!^{(0)}}f.
Thus our bounds extend (and slightly strengthen) the previous results.

7.5.
Geometric characterization of
\mathscr{A}
-determinacy

Given a map
f\in Maps(X,\!Y),
the support of
T^{1}_{\mathscr{A}}f
is the instability locus,
V(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}})\subseteq Crit(f)\subset X
. The ideal
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cap f^{\#}(y)\subset R_{Y}
defines the image

f(V(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}))\subset(\Bbbk^{p},o).
The ideal

R_{X}(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\cap f^{\#}(y))+\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}
defines the preimage,

f^{-1}(f(V(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}})))\cap Crit(f)
. This preimage contains the instability locus.
If
f
is of finite singularity type (i.e. the restriction
f:Crit\to(\Bbbk^{p},o)
is finite), then the loci
V(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}),

f^{-1}(f(V(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}})))\cap Crit(f)
have the same dimension.
Moreover, the two loci often coincide.

Corollary
7.6
extends the classical determinacy criterion to the generality of
{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk[[x]]$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$J$}\right.},\!{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk\{x\}$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$J$}\right.},\!{\footnotesize\left.\raisebox{1.37775pt}{$\Bbbk\langle x\rangle$}\!/\!\raisebox{-1.37775pt}{$J$}\right.}
.
Let
\mathfrak{a}\subset R_{X}
be the defining ideal of the locus
f^{-1}(f(V(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}})))\cap Crit(f).

Corollary 7.11
.

Assume
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}\!\subseteq\!{\mathfrak{m}}.

​1.​​

The map
f\!:X\!\to\!(\Bbbk^{p},o)
is
\mathscr{A}
-determined by its finite jet on the locus
f^{-1}(f(V(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}})))\!\cap\!Crit(f).
Namely,
\mathscr{A}f\!\supset\!\{f\}\!+\!\mathfrak{a}^{d}\!\cdot\!R^{\oplus p}_{X},
for
d\!\gg\!1,
where
\mathfrak{a}\!\subset\!R_{X}
is the defining ideal of
f^{-1}(f(V(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}})))\cap Crit(f).

​2.​​

If
f
is
\mathscr{A}
-finite (i.e.
\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}}={\mathfrak{m}}
), then
f
is
\mathscr{A}
-finitely determined.

​3.​​

Suppose
f
is
{\mathscr{K}}\!
-finite. Take the filtration
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\bullet}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}.

Suppose the local ring
\Bbbk
contains a field, and
char(\Bbbk)=0
or
char(\Bbbk)\gg 1
. If
\mathscr{A}^{(1)}f\supseteq\{f\}+\mathfrak{a}^{d}\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}
then
T_{\mathscr{A}}f\supseteq(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{R}}+\mathfrak{a}^{d})\cdot R^{\oplus p}_{X}.

In particular,
Supp(T^{1}_{\mathscr{A}}f)\subseteq V(\mathfrak{a})\subset X
, i.e.
f
is infinitesimally stable over
X\setminus V(\mathfrak{a})
.

For complex analytic maps,
f:(\mathbb{C}^{n},o)\to(\mathbb{C}^{p},o),
with isolated instabilities (i.e.
V(\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}})\!=\!o\in(\mathbb{C}^{n},o)
) this gives the classical finite determinacy criterion of Mather-Gaffney.

In the case
\sqrt{\mathfrak{a}_{\mathscr{A}}}\subsetneq{\mathfrak{m}}
, i.e.
f
has a non-isolated instability, this result seems to be new even for
\mathbb{C}\{x\}
.

Proof.
Parts 1 and 2 follow by Corollary
7.6
.
Part 3 follows by Corollary
7.9
.

\blacksquare
